At q=zeta/D, zeta>1, inversion of the whole block\n[[zeta/D,-1],[-1,D]] gives the exact Schur expenditure D*u^2/4:\nthe numerator is D*u^2*(zeta-1)/4 and its determinant is zeta-1.\nThus the required residual is the COMPLETE matrix\n\n    R78=C78-D*nu78*nu78^T/4,  R78 e=0.               (6.2)\n\nIndeed the entire polynomial has the identity\n\n    M78(q)=||x_0-(D/2)sum_i nu78_i g_i||^2/D\n       +sum_(i,j)(R78)_ij<g_i,g_j>\n       +(q-1/D)||x_0||^2.                           (6.3)\n\nConversely any negative R78 direction can have its common component\nshifted to make sum nu78_i g_i=0, without changing R78; setting x_0=0\nthen gives a negative value at EVERY q. This explains why merely\nraising q cannot repair a fixed exactly contacting array. It does\nnot require arbitrary alternative arrays to preserve exact contact.\n\nThere is also no omitted terminal cost. The pairs entering n have\nsources a=n-2^l,0<=l<m. Let Tin be the sum of their Tforward values.\nThe base terminal diagonal is1 and has no incoming rows. Full flow(3.1)\ntherefore makes the actual new terminal gradient diagonal\n\n    gamma78=1+Tin>0.\n\nWriting C78 for(3.3) with the explicit coefficients(5.4), set\n\n    vterm78=(nu78_n/2)x_0-sum_(i<n)(C78)_(i,n)g_i.\n\nThe exact terminal completion is\n\n    M78(q)=gamma78*||g_n-vterm78/gamma78||^2\n     +q||x_0||^2-sum_(i<n)nu78_i<x_0,g_i>\n     +sum_(i,j<n)(C78)_ij<g_i,g_j>\n     -||vterm78||^2/gamma78.                         (6.4)\n\nAll remaining coefficients in(6.4) are given by(3.3),(4.3),(4.5),(5.4).\nAn incoming-terminal-free square theorem is NOT applied to this altered\narray. Equations(6.2)--(6.4) retain all initial, common, terminal and\ncross-block expenditures. The failures below are evaluations of this\nwhole polynomial, before any coordinate is removed.\n\n## 7. Law I: the first original gradient is already negative\n\nTake x_0=0, g_0=1 and every other original gradient zero, including g_n.\nFor a pair0->v, write ell=P_v>0. Then y_0=-1, y_v=-b, where b=h_0\nis the physical first step from Section4.\nEquations(5.1)--(5.2) give\n\n    Delta_(0,target)=-delta*[1+(b-1)*2S/ell]<0.      (7.1)\n\nEvery pair not containing0 contributes zero, regardless of its position\nvectors, since g_a-g_b=0. There is no pair ending at0. The adjacent\npair(0,1) belongs to E78 and has ell=b, delta=vartheta78*2b/D.\nUsing the EXACT base diagonal(4.8), the complete value obeys\n\n    M_I(q;g_0=1,x_0=0)\n      <=(2/D)*[1-vartheta78*((b-1)D+1)]<0.          (7.2)\n\nThe final sign holds for every m>=1536: b-1>2/5,\nvartheta78=1/(4m^2), and D>=2^(m+1)-1>10m^2. For the last elementary\ninequality, it holds at m=16, and doubling2^(m+1)-1 and adding1\npreserves it because2m^2>=(m+1)^2 for m>=3. No recurrence was evaluated\nby a program. The inequality is therefore far inside its stated domain.\n\nThis is an original gradient diagonal with x_0=0, so changing q does\nnot alter the negative value. All final coefficients remain nonnegative,\nall value flow and Huber contact equations hold, and the optimum profile\nhas changed. The PSD conclusion nevertheless fails. The mechanism is\nspecific: paying even a small FRACTION of an optimum row to a nearby\nquery requires contact-balancing finite weights of order2S/ell.\nThose weights consume the first-gradient diagonal.\n\n## 8. Law II fixes that diagonal, but the full deep-wave test fails\n\nFor Law II, Tforward=vartheta78*mu_a and\nTbackward=Tforward*w78_b/w78_a. On the first-gradient test, each of\nits m outgoing pairs has value\n\n    vartheta78*mu_0*(-b+w78_b/w78_0).\n\nTherefore its entire diagonal is at least\n\n    (2/D)-epsilon*mu_0*b\n      =(2/D)*(1-epsilon*b^2)>0,                     (8.1)\n\nsince b^2<2. Thus the second law repairs the precise diagonal failure\nof Law I. A different full-space test is required.\n\nWe derive the inherited negative wave, including ALL depths, here.\nLet pterm_j=Psrc_j with only its terminal gradient1, and define\n\n    Fterm_j=A_j^2(1+t_j)-2A_j+(lambda+1).\n\nIn(4.4) take Y=-(A_j-1),Z=-1. Expansion of the bracket is Fterm_j.\nThus the finite expression is\n\n    pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(tstar-t_l)Fterm_l\n                                product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v. (8.2)\n\nAt depth1 pterm_1=2-b^2<3epsilon. The formulas(4.2) give\nk_1<7,0<tstar-t_1<epsilon,Fterm_1<13, whence\npterm_2<34epsilon<2epsilon*A_2^2. The depth2-to3 new term is\nnegative. Its normalized old coefficient is strictly between0 and1,\nso pterm_3/A_3^2<2epsilon even if the old coefficient is negative.\n\nFor l>=3, monotonicity and the exact t relation give\n\n    t_3>c*tstar/(2+tstar)=(1+epsilon)*tstar,\n    t_l-tstar>(2/5)epsilon.\n\nSince A_l>=8 and t_l>2/5, Fterm_l>A_l^2. Together with\nA_(l+1)<(5/2)A_l this yields\n\n    (t_l-tstar)Fterm_l/A_(l+1)^2>(8/125)epsilon.\n\nThe propagation multiplier is exactly\n\n    k_l/(2+t_l)^2\n      =1-(t_l-tstar)(1+t_l)/(2+t_l)^2,\n    1-epsilon<k_l/(2+t_l)^2<1, l>=2.                (8.3)\n\nUse (1+t)/(2+t)^2<=1/4 and t_l-tstar<lambda epsilon<5epsilon/2\nfor the lower inequality. Every product of at most m such factors\nis greater than (1-epsilon)^m>=1-m epsilon=3/4; the last inequality\nfollows by induction on the integer power. Unrolling(8.2) with these\nbounds retains every negative contribution and gives\n\n    pterm_j/A_j^2<[2-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon,\n                                      3<=j<=m.     (8.4)\n\nNow take j=m-3, r=2^j-1 and Hstep=h_r=1+d_j. On the FULL original\nscalar formal space assign\n\n    x_0=0, g_0=-Hstep/b, g_r=1,\n    g_i=0 for every other i, including the actual terminal n. (8.5)\n\nThe actual formal displacement is b*(-Hstep/b)+Hstep=0. All later\nformal positions vanish. In the first H_j there is no primitive cross\ncoefficient between its first and terminal gradients: g_0 enters only\nthe first depth1 bracket of(4.4), while its final terminal enters disjoint\nbrackets. The first diagonal is2-b^2 and the final is pterm_j. The next\njoin has Y=0,Z=1 and contributes\n\n    -Edef_j, Edef_j=(t_j-tstar)A_j^2(lambda+t_j)>0.\n\nAll larger primitive brackets have Y=Z=0. At the root objective join,\nx_p=g_p=T_R=0. Consequently the ENTIRE base polynomial at any real q is\n\n    D*Mbase(q;8.5)\n      =(2-b^2)(Hstep/b)^2+pterm_j-Edef_j.            (8.6)\n\nHere Hstep/(b A_j)<1/2, since A_j>5,d_j<A_j/2,b>7/5.\nCombining(8.4),(8.6) and2-b^2<3epsilon, and discarding only the\nstrictly negative -Edef_j, proves\n\n    Mbase(q;8.5)\n      <[11/4-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon*A_j^2/D.          (8.7)\n\nNo global-memory statement was used as a premise in this derivation.\n\nIt remains to bound EVERY Law II row increment, including terminal rows.\nThe complete proximal coordinates of(8.5) are\n\n    y_0=Hstep/b,\n    y_i=Hstep for1<=i<r,\n    y_r=Hstep-1,\n    y_i=0 for i>r.\n\nFor each dyadic pair u<v the increment is\n\n    vartheta78*mu_u*(g_u-g_v)\n                      *(y_v-(w78_v/w78_u)*y_u).    (8.8)\n\nAll possible nonzero cases are the following; they exhaust every pair:\n\n* u=0,v<r: the increment is negative, since\n  y_v-(w78_v/w78_0)y_0>=Hstep(1-1/h_0)>0.\n* u=0,v=r: the increment is negative, since\n  Hstep-1-Hstep/h_0>0; Hstep>7 and h_0>7/5 suffice.\n* u=0,v>r: it is nonnegative and at most\n  vartheta78*mu_0*Hstep^2/h_0^2. There are at most m such pairs.\n* 0<u<r,v=r: it equals\n  vartheta78*mu_u*[1-Hstep*(P_r-P_u)/w78_u]\n  and is at most vartheta78*mu_u.\n* u=r,v>r: it is negative.\n* Neither endpoint is0 or r: it is exactly zero.\n\nThe sum over all positive contributions is therefore bounded by\n\n    M_II(q;8.5)-Mbase(q;8.5)\n       <=2epsilon*Hstep^2/(bD)+2epsilon/m.          (8.9)\n\nThis uses mu_0=2b/D, sum mu=2, and at most m source0 pairs. It DOES\nNOT discard positive remote or terminal contributions. For A_j>=10,\nHstep/A_j<3/5 and b>7/5, so2Hstep^2/(bA_j^2)<18/35<1.\nThere are exactly three remaining SAME-m joins; hence\nA_m<(5/2)^3 A_j and D<(125/4)A_j. In particular\n2D/(m A_j^2)<125/(2m A_j)<1. Equation(8.9) is strictly less than\n2epsilon*A_j^2/D. Combining with(8.7), j=m-3, yields the full-space bound\n\n    M_II(q;8.5)\n      <[19/4-(6/125)(m-6)]epsilon*A_(m-3)^2/D<0,\n                       every m>=1536 and EVERY real q. (8.10)\n\nThe final numerical constants are exact rational inequalities; no\nfloating-point calculation or executed recurrence establishes them.\nLaw II has all signs, all original flow, all exact contact equations,\na changed optimum profile, and the positive first-gradient diagonal(8.1).\nIt still fails PSD in the full original space by(8.10).\n\n## 9. Scope of the two failures and the remaining interface\n\nThe two concrete laws(5.3) are excluded in this joint UNVERIFIED draft.\nTheir common mechanism was contact-preserving transport of original\noptimum mass across dyadic paired queries, with explicit paid debits.\nLaw I pays too much near the initial query because its finite-row mass\ncontains the physical factor2S/(P_b-P_a). No assertion is made that the raw\nfinite arrays belong to either of these laws. No exact sparsity follows.\n\nIn the full stored matrix the complete root-left zero-displacement\nrestriction, using an orthonormal basis but preserving its original-space\nembedding, has computed minimum eigenvalues0.001770064688887271,\n0.00017120692101997955,1.0911046137055806e-5. All individual tested\nfirst-gradient/bridge waves also have positive computed action; their full\ninitial and common couplings are exported. These tests do not certify the\nwhole matrix and occur only at m<=6, far outside the eventual domain1536.\n\nExactly THREE feature families were preregistered and fitted in the ONE\ncross-case job: physical source/destination features of finite rows;\ndyadic source/destination and containing-cell scale features of finite rows;\nand physical-prefix/step/level features of the optimum profile. No feature\nselection or additional fit was run. F1 finite-row relative errors were\n1.1807295211349638,0.9638567564479851,0.9878710345697729; F2 errors were\n2.163982775338135,0.8989173505036572,0.9575378600201162. F1 is rank9\nwith10 columns because its signed distance column is a linear combination\nof the physical-prefix columns; its least-squares coefficients are not\nunique. F3 errors were0.299062929376627,0.2538239721973367,\n0.44042110387348976. None supplies an exact law. In particular the two laws\nbelow are NOT fitted coefficients masquerading as a certificate.\n\n## 3. Complete original-space algebra\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All n+2 vectors\n(x_0,g_0,...,g_n) remain independent formal variables, including the\nunqueried terminal gradient. Put\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=sum h_t, D=1+2S, h_n=0,\n    y_i=x_i-g_i,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLet K_ij, i!=j finite, be all finite-row multipliers, nu_i the\nQ_(i,star) weights, and xi_i the Q_(star,i) weights. Diagonals are zero.\nValue cancellation is exactly\n\n    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xi_i=2*1_(i=n).         (3.1)\n\nAfter this cancellation the full polynomial is\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-sum_i nu_i<x_0,g_i>+C(g),\n    C(g)=sum_(i,j) C_ij<g_i,g_j>.                     (3.2)\n\nDefine out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij. Every entry of C is\n\n    C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                       +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (3.3)\n\nIndeed half the incident row mass is out_i-1_(i=n), by(3.1).\nOnly a forward row sourced at i contributes -h_i K_iv to its diagonal.\nFor i<j the difference squares give the first term, rows out of i landing\nafter j give the second, and rows out of j landing at/before i or at the\noptimum give the third. This derives the entire matrix, not a restriction.\nFor q>0 the exact initial Schur expenditure is nu nu^T/(4q).\n\nIn the analytic laws xi=0 and sum nu=2. Set e=(1,...,1).\nFull flow then gives e^T C e=D. On the whole Huber contact tuple\n(x_0,g)=(D,e), all finite and outgoing-optimum Q rows vanish, and\n\n    M(q;D,e)=(qD-1)D.                                (3.4)\n\nThere is no incoming-optimum Huber price in either proposed law. Their\nstronger derivative contact identity Ce=D nu/2 will be proved, not assumed.\nThe initial/common block is therefore [[q,-1],[-1,D]]. At21/(20D)\nits determinant is1/20. This block alone does not establish full PSD.\n\n## 4. The inherited base, rederived within this joint draft\n\nFix an integer m>=1536 and the SAME enclosing parameters at EVERY depth:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D_j=2A_j-1,\n    d_j=a*c^j, t_j=d_j/A_j, tstar=1/lambda.\n\nThe final h=H_m has n=n_m and D=D_m. These are mathematical definitions;\nnone of these recurrences was executed in this invocation. The finite\nsum formula for masses is\n\n    v_h=a/(c-2), A_j=v_h*c^j+(1-v_h)*2^j,\n    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                   (4.1)\n\nIt follows from c(c-2)>1>beta that0<v_h<1. Thus A_j>=2^j,\nt_j increases strictly, and t_j<c-2<1/2. With b=h_0=1+a,\n\n    b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n    A_1=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)], d_1=1-epsilon,\n    A_2=lambda^2-epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))],\n    d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),\n    lambda*d_2-A_2\n      =epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda^2 epsilon]>0.\n\nAlso lambda*d_1-A_1<0. Consequently\n\n    7/5<b<sqrt(2), A_1>12/5, A_2>5,\n    t_0,t_1<tstar<t_2,\n    0<t_j-tstar<lambda*epsilon for j>=2.              (4.2)\n\nFor the rational signs use epsilon<=1/6144, lambda^2<6 and\nlambda>241/100; the displayed subtractions are smaller than epsilon.\nThe exact relations A_(j+1)=(2+t_j)A_j and\nt_(j+1)=c*t_j/(2+t_j) also follow directly from the word.\n\nHere is EVERY primitive finite coefficient. Start Lambda^0=0. In a\njoin of two depth-j children let p=n_j, z=n_(j+1), and let i run over\nthe nonterminal right queries. Embed the left Lambda^j with weight1\nand the right with weight k_j. Add\n\n    i->p: u_j h_i, i->z: v_j h_i, z->p: w_j, p->z: z_j,\n    u_j=2lambda,\n    v_j=[2lambda d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,\n    k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j,\n    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-2lambda(A_j-1), z_j=2A_j.           (4.3)\n\nEquivalently each original coefficient is the sum of its indicated local\ncontributions over every dyadic node, multiplied by k_l for each ancestor\nwhose right child contains that node; left ancestor weights are1. This\nfinite sum/product is an explicit law, not an optimization or executed\nrecurrence. For w_j write2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda);(4.2) proves its\npositivity for j>=2, while w_0=4+2a and w_1>2lambda-2. All other signs\nare positive from their formulas. The identities\n\n    u_j+v_j=2(k_j-1),\n    u_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j)\n\ngive divergence -2h_i at a nonterminal and2S_j at the terminal.\nDefine the value-cancelled primitive polynomial\n\n    V_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))\n      +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n      -A_j(A_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2,\n    Psrc_j=V_j+sum Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.\n\nFor r_R=sum_(right nonterminal i)h_i g_i, put\nY=r_R-(A_j-1)g_z and Z=g_p-g_z. Expanding the four row types in(4.3)\nbefore their weights gives the following gradient expressions:\n\n    d_j<r_R,g_p>+||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2\n                                    +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;\n    -||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2\n                    +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r_R,g_z>;\n    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r_R>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;\n    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r_R>-<g_p,g_z>.\n\nCollecting these terms together with V and the two children yields\n\n    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right\n      +(tstar-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A_j<Y,Z>\n                        +A_j^2(2+tstar+t_j)||Z||^2]. (4.4)\n\nIn particular the pre-collection coefficients of r_R^2,r_R*g_p,r_R*g_z\nare (u_j-v_j)/2,u_j*d_j-z_j,w_j-v_j, verifying the mixed-term signs.\nThe bracket has positive first diagonal and determinant divided by A_j^2\nat least lambda>0. Positive epsilon therefore DOES NOT make these\nprimitive children PSD. That is never assumed below.\n\nThe original objective base is Kbase^0=0, mu^0_0=2, xi=0. At a join\nset A=S_j, Bjoin=1+d_j, Dp=D_(j+1), Dc=D_j, and\ns_j=(A+Bjoin)/(3A+2Bjoin), theta_j=1-s_j. With p=n_j, L_l=l and\nR_i=n_j+1+i, define\n\n    Kbase_(L_l,L_v)=Lambda^j_lv/Dp,\n    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv,\n    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mu^j_i,\n    mu_(L_l)=2h_l/Dp for l<n_j,\n    mu_p=2s_j-2A/Dp,\n    mu_(R_i)=theta_j mu^j_i.                         (4.5)\n\nAll other entries are zero; coincident contributions are summed. For a\nfully unrolled description the right-spine depth-l node starts at\n2^r-2^l. Its left primitive block is Lambda^(l-1)/D_l; each right query\nfeeds its bridge with s_(l-1) times that right child's explicitly defined\nmu. A query's optimum weight is2h_i/D_l if it is in that node's left\nnonterminal block, or2s_(l-1)-2S_(l-1)/D_l if it is its bridge, multiplied\nby product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t. The final terminal weight is\n2product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t. Each query has exactly one such category.\nTogether with(4.3), these are finite original-index formulas for all rows.\n\nThe bridge sign follows because\n(A+Bjoin)Dp-A(3A+2Bjoin)=A^2+4A*Bjoin+2Bjoin^2+A+Bjoin>0.\nAlso0<s_j<1 and theta_j>0. The primitive divergence cancels its left\noptimum rows; the bridge divergence is2s_j, canceled by the incoming\nright weights of total2s_j. Every right query's old flow is unchanged\nsince s_j+theta_j=1. Thus full flow(3.1) holds and sum mu=2.\nAll mu_i are strictly positive. The base has no incoming terminal rows.\n\nWrite T_R=sum_i mu^j_i g_(R_i),\nx_p=x_0-sum_(l<n_j)h_l g_(L_l),\nRchild=Cbase^j(g_R)-Dc||T_R||^2/4,\nand kappa_j=Dc-Dp theta_j^2. All original rows(4.5) give the identity\n\n    Mbase_(j+1)(1/Dp)=Psrc_j(g_L)/Dp+Rchild\n     +||x_p+(A-Dp s_j)g_p-(Dp theta_j/2)T_R||^2/Dp\n     +(kappa_j/4)||T_R-2g_p||^2.                    (4.6)\n\nFor an independent coefficient audit the remaining polynomial after\nsubtracting Psrc/Dp and Rchild is\n\n    ||x_p||^2/Dp+2(A/Dp-s_j)<x_p,g_p>\n    +(A^2/Dp+2s_j)||g_p||^2-theta_j<x_p,T_R>\n    +(Bjoin-s_j)<g_p,T_R>+Dc||T_R||^2/4.\n\nExpanding the two squares in(4.6) agrees term by term because\ns_j(3A+2Bjoin)=A+Bjoin. No child PSD or sign of kappa is needed for\nthis identity. At x_0=Dp and all gradients1, x_p=Dp-A and T_R=2;\nevery square argument is zero. The bracket in(4.4) has Y=Z=0.\nInduction from ||x_0-g_0||^2 shows zero value AND zero derivative:\n\n    Cbase^j e=D_j mu^j/2.                           (4.7)\n\nTwo further entries used below follow without any numerical computation:\n\n    mu_0=2b/D,    Cbase_00=2/D.                     (4.8)\n\nHere Cbase_00 denotes its first GRADIENT diagonal. In fact only the\nfirst depth1 primitive bracket contributes when g_0=1 and every other\ngradient is0; it contributes2-b^2. At the root objective join x_p=-b,\ng_p=T_R=0. The x_p square contributes b^2/D, so(4.8) follows.\n\n## 5. Exact paid two-row transport and the two coefficient laws\n\nWrite w78_i=2S-P_i, so S<=w78_i<=2S and every w78_i>0. Define the\nfixed set E78 of dyadic matched pairs as follows. For each depth\n1<=l<=m, each offset o=k*2^l with0<=k<2^(m-l), and\n0<=r<2^(l-1), take\n\n    a78=o+r, b78=o+r+2^(l-1).\n\nThese are exactly bit-flip edges of the m-dimensional binary cube on\nall2^m original gradient indices0,...,n, oriented toward the larger\nindex. Both child terminal indices participate. Every source belongs\nto at most m pairs, and index0 belongs to exactly m. Distinct pairs\nare counted once. This is a sparse, explicit dyadic law; it is not a\nseparable product kernel or an inferred numerical support.\n\nFor one pair a78<b78 abbreviate a0=a78, b0=b78 in THIS paragraph only,\nell=P_b0-P_a0>0, wa=w78_a0, wb=w78_b0, so wa-wb=ell.\nFor a prescribed transfer delta>=0 set\n\n    Tforward=delta*wa/ell, Tbackward=delta*wb/ell.     (5.1)\n\nAdd Tforward to K_(a0,b0) and Tbackward to K_(b0,a0), DECREASE the\noriginal Q_(a0,star) multiplier by delta, and INCREASE the original\nQ_(b0,star) multiplier by delta. The net value flow is zero at every\nnode because Tforward-Tbackward=delta. This is a paid change of the\noptimum profile, not an additive balanced finite circulation.\n\nThe exact complete polynomial increment is\n\n    Delta_ab=Tforward*(Q_ab+Q_(b,star)-Q_(a,star))\n             +Tbackward*(Q_ba+Q_(a,star)-Q_(b,star))\n       =<g_a-g_b,Tforward*y_b-Tbackward*y_a>.        (5.2)\n\nTo check the basic identity, expand the three Q rows. Their function\nvalues cancel. The position terms equal<g_a-g_b,x_b>, and the\ndifference-square and optimum-square terms sum to\n||g_b||^2-<g_a,g_b>. Hence Q_ab+Q_(b,star)-Q_(a,star)\n=<g_a-g_b,y_b>. This proves(5.2) without an external theorem.\n\nFor BOTH laws put vartheta78=epsilon/m. Use the original base mu from\n(4.5), without funding a debit from newly received mass. The ONLY TWO\nchoices of transfer are\n\n    Law I:  delta_ab=vartheta78*mu_a;\n    Law II: delta_ab=vartheta78*mu_a*(P_b-P_a)/w78_a. (5.3)\n\nNo additional law or tuned parameter search is part of this invocation.\nLaw II is the prescribed physical-distance attenuation of Law I.\nFor every original i,j the final coefficients are explicitly\n\n    K78_ij=Kbase_ij\n      +sum_((a,b) in E78)[1_(i=a,j=b)Tforward_ab\n                        +1_(i=b,j=a)Tbackward_ab],\n    nu78_i=mu_i-sum_((i,b) in E78)delta_ib\n                  +sum_((a,i) in E78)delta_ai,\n    xi78_i=0, K78_ii=0.                             (5.4)\n\nEvery decrement is paid: in either law delta_ab<=vartheta78 mu_a,\nso the total outgoing debit at node a is at most epsilon*mu_a.\nThus nu78_i>=(1-epsilon)mu_i>0, and every finite coefficient is\nnonnegative. Incoming optimum multipliers are explicitly zero as a\nchoice of these laws, not a deletion theorem. The total gross optimum\nmass moved over the ENTIRE word is at most2epsilon, since sum mu=2.\nOutgoing optimum mass remains exactly2. Law II adds at most4epsilon\nfinite multiplier mass because Tforward=vartheta78 mu_a and\n0<Tbackward<=Tforward. Law I's exact finite added mass is\nsum_(E78)delta_ab(wa+wb)/ell; it is not silently assumed small.\nAll added incoming and outgoing terminal rows remain part of(5.4).\n\n## 6. Whole-word contact, initial/common and terminal charges\n\nAt the original Huber contact tuple x_0=D, g_i=1,\n\n    y_i=D-P_i-1=w78_i.\n\nBoth vector factors on the right side of(5.2) vanish:\ng_a-g_b=0 and Tforward*wb-Tbackward*wa=0. Consequently each increment\nhas zero value AND zero full derivative there, including its initial\ncoordinate. Combining this with(4.7) proves exactly\n\n    C78 e=D nu78/2.                                 (6.1)\n\nThe changes in nu are therefore accompanied by changes in the entire\ncontact row-sum vector; the old optimum profile is not frozen. There\nis ZERO incoming-optimum Huber cost, ZERO contact mismatch, and ZERO\nper-node or per-level scalar Huber allowance. The one whole-word\ninitial/common block and its budget are precisely(3.4). At q=1/D\nits kernel is(D,1); at q=21/(20D) it has determinant1/20. Neither\nstatement certifies the transverse space.\n\nFor completeness, let v be any gradient column with e^T v=0, and\nwrite u=nu78^T v. Its coupling to the initial/common coordinates is\n(-u/2,Du/2). At q=zeta/D, zeta>1, inversion of the whole block\n[[zeta/D,-1],[-1,D]] gives the exact Schur expenditure D*u^2/4:\nthe numerator is D*u^2*(zeta-1)/4 and its determinant is zeta-1.\nThus the required residual is the COMPLETE matrix\n\n    R78=C78-D*nu78*nu78^T/4,  R78 e=0.               (6.2)\n\nIndeed the entire polynomial has the identity\n\n    M78(q)=||x_0-(D/2)sum_i nu78_i g_i||^2/D\n       +sum_(i,j)(R78)_ij<g_i,g_j>\n       +(q-1/D)||x_0||^2.                           (6.3)\n\nConversely any negative R78 direction can have its common component\nshifted to make sum nu78_i g_i=0, without changing R78; setting x_0=0\nthen gives a negative value at EVERY q. This explains why merely\nraising q cannot repair a fixed exactly contacting array. It does\nnot require arbitrary alternative arrays to preserve exact contact.\n\nThere is also no omitted terminal cost. The pairs entering n have\nsources a=n-2^l,0<=l<m. Let Tin be the sum of their Tforward values.\nThe base terminal diagonal is1 and has no incoming rows. Full flow(3.1)\ntherefore makes the actual new terminal gradient diagonal\n\n    gamma78=1+Tin>0.\n\nWriting C78 for(3.3) with the explicit coefficients(5.4), set\n\n    vterm78=(nu78_n/2)x_0-sum_(i<n)(C78)_(i,n)g_i.\n\nThe exact terminal completion is\n\n    M78(q)=gamma78*||g_n-vterm78/gamma78||^2\n     +q||x_0||^2-sum_(i<n)nu78_i<x_0,g_i>\n     +sum_(i,j<n)(C78)_ij<g_i,g_j>\n     -||vterm78||^2/gamma78.                         (6.4)\n\nAll remaining coefficients in(6.4) are given by(3.3),(4.3),(4.5),(5.4).\nAn incoming-terminal-free square theorem is NOT applied to this altered\narray. Equations(6.2)--(6.4) retain all initial, common, terminal and\ncross-block expenditures. The failures below are evaluations of this\nwhole polynomial, before any coordinate is removed.\n\n## 7. Law I: the first original gradient is already negative\n\nTake x_0=0, g_0=1 and every other original gradient zero, including g_n.\nFor a pair0->v, write ell=P_v>0. Then y_0=-1, y_v=-b, where b=h_0\nis the physical first step from Section4.\nEquations(5.1)--(5.2) give\n\n    Delta_(0,target)=-delta*[1+(b-1)*2S/ell]<0.      (7.1)\n\nEvery pair not containing0 contributes zero, regardless of its position\nvectors, since g_a-g_b=0. There is no pair ending at0. The adjacent\npair(0,1) belongs to E78 and has ell=b, delta=vartheta78*2b/D.\nUsing the EXACT base diagonal(4.8), the complete value obeys\n\n    M_I(q;g_0=1,x_0=0)\n      <=(2/D)*[1-vartheta78*((b-1)D+1)]<0.          (7.2)\n\nThe final sign holds for every m>=1536: b-1>2/5,\nvartheta78=1/(4m^2), and D>=2^(m+1)-1>10m^2. For the last elementary\ninequality, it holds at m=16, and doubling2^(m+1)-1 and adding1\npreserves it because2m^2>=(m+1)^2 for m>=3. No recurrence was evaluated\nby a program. The inequality is therefore far inside its stated domain.\n\nThis is an original gradient diagonal with x_0=0, so changing q does\nnot alter the negative value. All final coefficients remain nonnegative,\nall value flow and Huber contact equations hold, and the optimum profile\nhas changed. The PSD conclusion nevertheless fails. The mechanism is\nspecific: paying even a small FRACTION of an optimum row to a nearby\nquery requires contact-balancing finite weights of order2S/ell.\nThose weights consume the first-gradient diagonal.\n\n## 8. Law II fixes that diagonal, but the full deep-wave test fails\n\nFor Law II, Tforward=vartheta78*mu_a and\nTbackward=Tforward*w78_b/w78_a. On the first-gradient test, each of\nits m outgoing pairs has value\n\n    vartheta78*mu_0*(-b+w78_b/w78_0).\n\nTherefore its entire diagonal is at least\n\n    (2/D)-epsilon*mu_0*b\n      =(2/D)*(1-epsilon*b^2)>0,                     (8.1)\n\nsince b^2<2. Thus the second law repairs the precise diagonal failure\nof Law I. A different full-space test is required.\n\nWe derive the inherited negative wave, including ALL depths, here.\nLet pterm_j=Psrc_j with only its terminal gradient1, and define\n\n    Fterm_j=A_j^2(1+t_j)-2A_j+(lambda+1).\n\nIn(4.4) take Y=-(A_j-1),Z=-1. Expansion of the bracket is Fterm_j.\nThus the finite expression is\n\n    pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(tstar-t_l)Fterm_l\n                                product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v. (8.2)\n\nAt depth1 pterm_1=2-b^2<3epsilon. The formulas(4.2) give\nk_1<7,0<tstar-t_1<epsilon,Fterm_1<13, whence\npterm_2<34epsilon<2epsilon*A_2^2. The depth2-to3 new term is\nnegative. Its normalized old coefficient is strictly between0 and1,\nso pterm_3/A_3^2<2epsilon even if the old coefficient is negative.\n\nFor l>=3, monotonicity and the exact t relation give\n\n    t_3>c*tstar/(2+tstar)=(1+epsilon)*tstar,\n    t_l-tstar>(2/5)epsilon.\n\nSince A_l>=8 and t_l>2/5, Fterm_l>A_l^2. Together with\nA_(l+1)<(5/2)A_l this yields\n\n    (t_l-tstar)Fterm_l/A_(l+1)^2>(8/125)epsilon.\n\nThe propagation multiplier is exactly\n\n    k_l/(2+t_l)^2\n      =1-(t_l-tstar)(1+t_l)/(2+t_l)^2,\n    1-epsilon<k_l/(2+t_l)^2<1, l>=2.                (8.3)\n\nUse (1+t)/(2+t)^2<=1/4 and t_l-tstar<lambda epsilon<5epsilon/2\nfor the lower inequality. Every product of at most m such factors\nis greater than (1-epsilon)^m>=1-m epsilon=3/4; the last inequality\nfollows by induction on the integer power. Unrolling(8.2) with these\nbounds retains every negative contribution and gives\n\n    pterm_j/A_j^2<[2-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon,\n                                      3<=j<=m.     (8.4)\n\nNow take j=m-3, r=2^j-1 and Hstep=h_r=1+d_j. On the FULL original\nscalar formal space assign\n\n    x_0=0, g_0=-Hstep/b, g_r=1,\n    g_i=0 for every other i, including the actual terminal n. (8.5)\n\nThe actual formal displacement is b*(-Hstep/b)+Hstep=0. All later\nformal positions vanish. In the first H_j there is no primitive cross\ncoefficient between its first and terminal gradients: g_0 enters only\nthe first depth1 bracket of(4.4), while its final terminal enters disjoint\nbrackets. The first diagonal is2-b^2 and the final is pterm_j. The next\njoin has Y=0,Z=1 and contributes\n\n    -Edef_j, Edef_j=(t_j-tstar)A_j^2(lambda+t_j)>0.\n\nAll larger primitive brackets have Y=Z=0. At the root objective join,\nx_p=g_p=T_R=0. Consequently the ENTIRE base polynomial at any real q is\n\n    D*Mbase(q;8.5)\n      =(2-b^2)(Hstep/b)^2+pterm_j-Edef_j. The chain's remote multiplier is0,\nbut all remote primal constraints from Section3 remain in force.\n\n## 5. Full terminal multipliers and complete quadratic certificate\n\nFor Family E set t=T. Add the single row Q_(n,R_0) with multiplier1\nto the core table. Equal r gradients make it\n\n                    f_n-F_r+T||r||^2=0.\n\nConsequently the total weighted row sum is\n\n                 f_n-<gbar,x_0>+Q(p,q,r).              (5.1)\n\nFor Family Q put N_-=n-1, s_n=g_n, and\n\n                    t=T-d0^2/2.                       (5.2)\n\nAdd Q_(N_-,R_0) and Q_(n,N_-) with multipliers1 each. If N_-=R_0,\nomit the coincident first row: its expression is identically zero\nand its flow path has length0. The actual data satisfy\n\n    f_(N_-)=F_r-(T-d0)||r||^2,\n    x_n=x_(N_-)-d0*r,\n    Q_(n,N_-)=f_n-f_(N_-)+d0<s_n,r>+||s_n-r||^2/2.\n\nCompleting the last expression shows that the total weighted row sum\nis EXACTLY\n\n    f_n-<gbar,x_0>+Q(p,q,r)\n                +||s_n+(d0-1)r||^2/2.                (5.3)\n\nThe value cancellation is complete: flow1 starts at n, passes through\nN_- and R_0 and the core paths, and ends at the chosen optimum. This\nderivation keeps the entire original prefix and the actual final\nentry. It is not a fictitious suffix, a change of objective after a\nquery, or an assumption about the free terminal gradient.\n\nIf mu_ij denotes the table plus the terminal-path multipliers, the\nFULL certificate for the normalized payoff has lambda_ij=2mu_ij and\n\n    ||x_0||^2/(2D)-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n      =2D||gbar-x_0/(2D)||^2\n        +2[J11||s||^2+2J12<s,tvec>+J22||tvec||^2]\n        + terminal_square,                            (5.4)\n\nwhere terminal_square is0 for E and ||s_n+(d0-1)r||^2 for Q.\nUse either coordinate basis specified in (4.5) or (4.8). ALL other\nordered multipliers are0; the flow at each non-optimum original sample\nis2 at n and0 elsewhere, including every omitted interior index.\nThese are certificates on the entire restricted equal-sector vector\nspace, with no additional equalities among x_0,p,q,r or s_n.\n\nIf D>0 and J is PSD, (5.4), Q_ij<=0 and the nonnegative multipliers\ngive 2f_n<=||x_0||^2/(2D). In particular no positive queried height\nhas been discarded or bounded without an original row. The positive\nterminal square permits every real terminal gradient. The original\nprefix can determine a completely different closest minimizer after\ncompletion: re-centering that completed function at that minimizer\nleaves this same proof applicable.\n\n## 6. Exact physical masses and uniform rational bounds\n\nThe recurrence for masses follows directly from concatenation:\n\n    X_(l+1)=2X_l+a*c^l, X_0=1,\n    X_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l, v_h=a/(c-2).             (6.1)\n\nThe closed form is checked by substitution; no recurrence is executed.\nFor m>=1536 the following inequalities hold:\n\n    12/5<c<29/12,\n    2/5<a<c-2<5/12,\n    0<v_h<1, X_l>=2^l,\n    a<=a*c^l/X_l<c-2 (l>0 for the strict upper bound),\n    7/5<d0<17/12<3/2,\n    2/5<d/U<5/12, U>=4096.                            (6.2)\n\nFor details, 7/5<sqrt(2)<99/70 follows by squaring. Therefore\nc>12/5 and c<(169/70)(6145/6144)<29/12. The latter rational\ncomparison reduces to2028<2*6144. Also beta_h>=6143/6144>99/100,\nso a>(99/100)(12/29)>2/5. Since lambda(lambda-2)=1,\nc(c-2)>1>beta_h and a<c-2. These imply 0<v_h<1.\nFormula (6.1), and 2/c<1, give a*c^l/X_l between a and c-2\n(with lower equality only at l=0). The lower bound on U follows\nfrom U>=2^(m-1) and m-1>=12. All bounds are exact rational ones.\n\nFor any k in (1.1), write the right child, with no resetting of m,\nas (block_1,1+e,block_2). Define\n\n    v=M+1, z=T+1,\n    e=a*c^ell_k, ell_k=nu_2(k+1),\n    epsilon_E=1/2, epsilon_Q=(1+d0^2)/2,\n    epsilon=epsilon_E or epsilon_Q,\n    w=z-epsilon, delta=epsilon/U, t_root=d/U.\n\nThe notation nu_2(N) means the greatest integer l>=0 with 2^l\ndividing the positive integer N. The actual index formula for h is\nh_i=1+a*c^(nu_2(i+1)), inherited from the binary concatenation.\nThe selected checkpoint's two complete child words have mass\nX_(ell_k)-1 each. Consequently\n\n    v+e+z=U,\n    v>=X_(ell_k), z>=X_(ell_k),\n    0<e<(5/12)min(v,z),\n    D=2U+d-epsilon,\n    0<epsilon<13/8, 0<delta<1/1000.                  (6.3)\n\nBecause k<=n-2, its suffix includes the actual final leaf d0.\nFor either family\n\n    w>=d0+1/2-d0^2/2=1-a^2/2>9/10,\n    e<(5/12)(w+epsilon)<(6/5)w.                       (6.4)\n\nFor the last strict inequality use epsilon/w<(13/8)/(9/10)=65/36,\nso e/w<505/432<6/5. No sign condition on a gradient is involved.\n\nIn both choices of flow, with u=U, set\n\n    D_bar=D/U=2+t_root-delta,\n    alpha=(1+t_root)/(3+2t_root-2delta).\n\nThe following bounds hold uniformly:\n\n    19/8 < D_bar < 29/12,\n    9/25 < alpha < 3/8,\n    J11=alpha(U-D*alpha) > (3/80)U,\n    0 < t_root-alpha < 3/50.                          (6.5)\n\nThe D bounds follow from (6.2)-(6.3). For alpha, its numerator is\nbetween7/5 and17/12, its denominator is below23/6 and above\n19/5-1/500. These give alpha>42/115>9/25 and alpha<3/8,\nsince17/12<(3/8)(19/5-1/500). To obtain the sharper J11 bound,\n\n    1-D_bar*alpha\n      =[1-t_root-t_root^2-delta(1-t_root)]\n         /(3+2t_root-2delta)\n      >(59/144-1/1000)/(23/6)>5/48.\n\nFor the last comparison, 59/144-115/288=1/96>1/1000.\nMultiply by alpha>9/25 to get J11/U>3/80.\nFinally t_root-alpha>2/5-3/8=1/40 and\nt_root-alpha<5/12-9/25=17/300<3/50.\n\nThe exact physical classification of the two certificate regions is\n\n    v<=U/2  if and only if  j<k<=k_R,\n    v> U/2  if and only if  k_R<k<=n-2.               (6.6)\n\nIndeed write X=X_(m-2), d_child=a*c^(m-2). Then U=2X+d_child.\nAt k=k_R the prefix mass is v=X<U/2, and every earlier k has\nv<=X. Immediately AFTER that physical root the mass is\nv=X+d_child+1>U/2; every later k has at least that mass since all\nsteps are positive. Thus (6.6) is a chronological dyadic\nclassification, not a test that must be run over pairs.\n\n## 7. Analytic PSD proof in both physical regions\n\n### Region I: j<k<=k_R, use the remote flow\n\nHere 1<=v<=U/2 and e<(5/12)v. From (4.6) and (6.3),\n\n    beta=(v+e)/(3U-2epsilon-2v), ell=beta.\n\nIts denominator is greater than (2-2/1000)U. Therefore\n\n    0<beta<(5/7)(v/U)<=5/14<9/25<alpha,\n    gamma=1-alpha-beta>1-3/8-5/14>0.                 (7.1)\n\nThe coefficient5/7 is valid because\n(17/12)/(2-2/1000)<5/7, equivalently119/12<999/100.\nThus every multiplier in the core table is nonnegative. Moreover\n\n    D*beta/v < (29/12)(5/7)=145/84<7/4,\n    J22=beta(2v-D*beta)>beta*v/4,\n    0<J12=beta*U*(t_root-alpha)/2<3beta*U/100.         (7.2)\n\nTogether with (6.5),\n\n    J11*J22-J12^2\n      > (3/320)beta*U*v-(9/10000)beta^2*U^2\n      > beta*U*v[3/320-9/14000] >0.                 (7.3)\n\nBoth diagonal entries are positive. Thus the entire 2-by-2\nremainder is positive definite. Explicitly it is\n\n    J11||s+(J12/J11)tvec||^2\n       +(J22-J12^2/J11)||tvec||^2,\n\nwith both coefficients positive. Equations (5.4) and (7.1)-(7.3)\ngive a nonnegative-multiplier full quadratic certificate, not merely\na determinant scan. In particular the root/next-leaf pair has v=1,\ne=a, T=U-2-a; every row and the ACTUAL leaf are exactly those in\nthe assigned problem.\n\n### Region II: k_R<k<=n-2, use the chain flow\n\nNow v>U/2. (7.5)\n\nWe prove it with two analytic subcases, not by sampling parameters.\n\nIf w<=U/4, (6.4) gives e+w<(11/5)w and e+2w<(16/5)w.\nTherefore\n\n    D-w>(17/8)U,\n    D*w-(e+w)^2\n      >[(19/8)-(121/100)]U*w=(233/200)U*w.\n\nThe left side of (7.5) is greater than\n\n    4(3/80)(17/8)(233/200)U^3 w\n                   =(11883/32000)U^3 w.\n\nThe right side is less than\n\n    (9/64)U^2(256/25)w^2\n                   <=(9/25)U^3 w.\n\nThe difference of the displayed coefficients is363/32000>0.\n\nIf w>=U/4, (6.3) improves the tail ratio to\n\n    e/w < (5/12)(1+4delta)<3/7.\n\nThus e+w<(10/7)w and e+2w<(17/7)w. As also e+w<U/2,\n\n    D-w>(15/8)U,\n    D*w-(e+w)^2>(19/8-5/7)U*w=(93/56)U*w.\n\nThe left side of (7.5) is greater than\n(4185/8960)U^3 w. The quantity e+2w is less than both U and\n(17/7)w, so the right side is less than\n\n                   (9/64)(17/7)U^3 w\n                         =(153/448)U^3 w.\n\nTheir coefficient difference is1125/8960>0. This establishes (7.5) for every physical Region II pair.\n\nRegions I and II exhaust (1.1). Both terminal charges were treated\nsimultaneously, with no changes to the real gradient geometry or\nto the complete endpoint constraints.\n\n## 8. Sharp legal member, its true radius, and COMPLETE K\n\nFor any nonnegative word of total sum S=S_m put kappa=1/(1+2S)\nand define the ONE global scalar function\n\n    f_H(x)=x^2/2                         (|x|<=kappa),\n    f_H(x)=kappa*|x|-kappa^2/2           (|x|>=kappa).\n\nIts derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. At every original cumulative\nstep mass s<=S,\n\n    x=1-kappa*s>kappa,\n    g=kappa, f_H(x)=kappa(1-kappa*s)-kappa^2/2.\n\nThis follows directly from kappa=1/(1+2S), S>0 and s<=S; all\nsteps are nonnegative. No assertion below is a new fact.\nAll new construction, algebra, dynamics, and comparisons are derived jointly.\n\nTwo finite, correlated, all-history laws are analyzed. They use one static\ncentered-simplex objective, exact coordinate counts, and an initial point fixed\nfrom the entire word before the forward run. They are not Cartesian copies of\nboth inverse branches over every future level.\n\n* **Law I:** select the two largest future coordinate levels when their gap\n  permits the two-coordinate inverse; otherwise select the largest alone.\n  Arbitrary N>=3, arbitrary scale, and arbitrary allowed inactive-coordinate\n  variations are included. The two upper coordinate identities persist, but\n  their actual positive support can leave and re-enter the edge. On EVERY\n  SAME-m H_(m,1/4), m>=1536, its whole domain satisfies\n\n      E <= 1/(2S-2),       B_n E < 19/20.                       (A)\n\n  The global Huber score 1/(1+2S) is attained within the family. Thus its\n  possible gain over that member is at most the relative factor\n  (1+2S)/(2S-2)=1+3/(2S-2). This does not prove E<=K_n or E<=F_ray_n.\n\n* **Law II:** replace each upper coordinate by r identical coordinates and\n  retain q>=1 inactive coordinates, N=2r+q. Every count is exact and finite.\n  Its transverse/carrier energy ratio is kappa=N/q, which is unbounded.\n  The same argument gives a precise deficit when kappa is not too large,\n  but a uniform extension of (A) would be unjustified. Over all finite r,q,\n  the exact supremum of this law is the worst-case profile of ONE scalar\n  symmetric Huber function over its initial point. This is a reduction, not\n  an evaluation of that scalar profile, and not an attained improvement.\n\nIn particular, the original all-word exact-profile target remains OPEN.\nThere is no claim about arbitrary simplex starts or arbitrary completions of\nthese prefixes. Only the static function actually constructed here is used.\n\n## 1. Target, benchmark, and accepted scopes\n\nFor a finite nonnegative word h of length n and L>0, the original W_n(h) is\nthe supremum of 2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over finite-dimensional\nconvex differentiable functions with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained\nminimum, with x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). The exact-profile question\nretains every n and every nonnegative word, including zero and very large\nsteps. A result about this one diagnostic family is not that theorem.\nNormalization here is W=2R.\n\nThe original all-integer-split denominators are\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    Gbench(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) Gbench(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                             (1)\n\nThese are definitions, not executed recurrences. Accepted facts used at\ntheir exact scopes are:\n\n* 1252b8823bb5a290: the full ordered interpolation convention and necessity;\n  no free-terminal optimization from it is used to change this objective.\n* b98bb807d514e80d: K_n includes the complete-word Huber member 1/(1+2S).\n* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: K_n<=F_ray_n. No converse or scalar-family completeness\n  statement is contained in that fact or asserted here.\n* 5fd396eba75651e7, Section10, equation(77), ONLY its stated benchmark bound\n  B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250 on H_(m,1/4), m>=1536.\n\nThe older f7f9930b98aa7caa, its Cartesian-width obstruction, and its first-leaf\ncounterexample were read for scope. None of their unverified mathematics is\nimported as a premise. The general inverse identity is rederived below.\n\nFix the diagnostic's SAME enclosing m throughout every child:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, S=S_m.                       (2)\n\nHere all h_t>1. Counting entries of each level gives\n\n    S_j+1=(1-v)2^j+v c^j, v=beta/[c(c-2)]=a/(c-2).            (3)\n\nFor m>=1536, 1/3<a<1/2, 2<c<5/2, beta>999/1000, and 0<v<1.\nIndeed c>lambda and lambda(lambda-2)=1 give v<1. The elementary bounds\nlambda>12/5, lambda<121/50, beta>999/1000 imply the displayed coarse\nestimates. Since c-2<1/2, v>2a, and consequently\n\n    a*c^j < S_j+1  for every j>=0.                            (4)\n\nAlso S>n>=m>=1536. No small-horizon or reset-parameter substitute is used.\n\n## 2. One finite real dictionary and its global smooth objective\n\nFor any integer N>=3 let\n\n    E_N={x in R^N:sum_j x_j=0}, u_N=(1/N,...,1/N), sigma>0,\n    v_j=sigma(e_j-u_N), D=conv{v_j:1<=j<=N},\n    F(x)=max_(g in D) {<x,g>-||g||^2/2}, x in E_N.            (5)\n\nAll offsets are zero. The slopes are real, fixed, and have Gram\n\n    <v_i,v_j>=sigma^2(1_(i=j)-1/N).                           (6)\n\nThis is an ordinary Euclidean space of dimension N-1. If literal coordinates\nin R^(N-1) are desired, use the orthonormal columns\nb_k=(e_1+...+e_k-k e_(k+1))/sqrt(k(k+1)), 1<=k<N, and their transpose.\nNo extra carrier coordinate or variable metric is added.\n\nThe maximizer is the unique nearest-point projection g=P_D(x). Its defining\nnormal inequalities are <x-g,v_j-g><=0 for every j. At two inputs, adding\nthe normal inequalities proves <x-x',g-g'> >= ||g-g'||^2, hence g is\n1-Lipschitz. Evaluating (5) at the two maximizers bounds the first-order\nremainder between zero and ||x-x'||^2. Thus F is finite everywhere, convex,\ndifferentiable, and grad F=P_D. Near zero the projection is x, because zero\nis interior to D in E_N. The least gradient Lipschitz constant is exactly1.\n\nIts COMPLETE minimum set is {0}, with value0. For nonzero x in E_N, some\nx_j>0, and a sufficiently small positive multiple of v_j lies in D and\ngives a positive expression in (5). Consequently the OWN closest radius is\nexactly ||x_0||, including all inactive-coordinate variation.\n\nWrite x=(sigma/N)w, sum w_j=0. Let theta be the unique threshold satisfying\n\n    (1/N)sum_j(1+w_j-theta)_+=1,\n    P_j=(1+w_j-theta)_+.\n\nThen\n\n    g_j=(sigma/N)(P_j-1),\n    y_j=(x-g)_j=(sigma/N)min(1+w_j,theta),\n    delta=<y,g>=sigma^2 theta/N,\n    2F(x)=(sigma^2/N)[2theta+(1/N)sum_j(P_j-1)^2].             (7)\n\nTo verify this directly, sum P_j=N implies sum min(1+w_j,theta)=0.\nEvery positive P_j has residual sigma*theta/N and every zero P_j has\nresidual no larger; this proves the normal inequality against ALL vertices.\nIt proves (7) without importing a projection theorem. The threshold is\nnonnegative because the positive-part average at theta0 is at least1.\n\nFor ANY two actual samples i,j, including the terminal and the optimum,\nexpansion of f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 gives\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                                  (8)\n\nThis proves every ordered remote, reversed, terminal, and optimum row on\nthe same static function. Repeated samples require no deletion. In\nparticular Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-delta_i<=0.\n\n## 3. General correlated inverse identity, jointly rederived\n\nFor h=1+d>1, the forward rule in w coordinates is\n\n    w'_j=w_j+h-h(1+w_j-theta)_+\n        =w_j+h             on an inactive coordinate,\n        =-d w_j+h theta    on an active coordinate.           (9)\n\nGiven a centered future list w', choose a proper nonempty set A and put\np=|A|/N and mu=(1/N)sum_(j in A)w'_j. Solving (9) coordinatewise and\nrequiring mean zero yields\n\n    theta=[mu+d(1-p)]/p,\n    w_j=w'_j-h                     if j outside A,\n    w_j=(h theta-w'_j)/d            if j in A.                (10)\n\nFor example, the mean of the candidates multiplied by d is\n-h*mu-dh(1-p)+ph*theta, which vanishes exactly for this theta.\nIf theta>0 and max_j w'_j<theta+d, then on A\n1+w_j-theta=(theta+d-w'_j)/d>0, while outside A it equals\nw'_j-(theta+d)<0. Their average positive part is\n[p(theta+d)-mu]/d=1. Thus (10) really is the positive-height inverse\nwith the prescribed exact support, not just a formal preimage.\n\nThe following two laws give concrete sets satisfying these guards at\nEVERY original index. Both branches are never independently duplicated.\n\n## 4. Both complete laws, with exact multiplicities and terminal data\n\nPartition the coordinates into G_+, G_-, and I with\n\n    |G_+|=|G_-|=r>=1, |I|=q>=1, N=2r+q.\n\nLaw I has r=1 and arbitrary q=N-2. Law II allows all positive integers\nr,q. Every coordinate in either upper group has the same value; coordinates\nin I may have unequal values. Define the fixed face averages and axes\n\n    c_+=sigma(1_(G_+)/r-u_N), c_-=sigma(1_(G_-)/r-u_N),\n    c_face=(c_++c_-)/2, alpha=||c_face||=sigma sqrt(q/(2rN)),\n    b=sigma/sqrt(2r), e=c_face/alpha,\n    u=(1_(G_+)-1_(G_-))/sqrt(2r),\n    kappa=b^2/alpha^2=N/q.                                   (11)\n\nThe vectors e,u are orthonormal. The vector called a carrier here is the\nactual barycenter c_face INSIDE E_N; it is not an added geometric direction.\nLet Z be zero on both upper groups, supported on I, with sum_(j in I)Z_j=0.\nIt is orthogonal to e,u. Choose any terminal scalar t in R, any\n\n    R>max{1,|t|},\n    max_(j in I) Z_j < [sigma/(2r)](R-max{1,|t|}).            (12)\n\nThe terminal point, fixed before the run, is\n\n    x_n=alpha R e+b t u+Z.                                  (13)\n\nThe Z=0 choice is always allowed. Its exact terminal projection is\n\n    g_n=alpha e+b clip(t)u, clip(s)=max(-1,min(1,s)).          (14)\n\nTo check (14), for |t|<1 the residual is constant on both upper groups,\nand comparison with an inactive residual is precisely\nZ_j<sigma(R-1)/(2r). For |t|>=1 the projection is c_+ or c_-;\nthe inactive residual condition is Z_j<=sigma(R+|t|-2)/(2r), which follows\nfrom the stronger ordering bound Z_j<sigma(R-|t|)/(2r). At |t|=1 the\nsame formulas agree. Thus all inactive coordinates are strictly lower,\nand the height is positive. These are tests against every original vertex.\n\nNow fix the whole backward scalar dictionary, with no forward oracle:\n\n    z_n=t, R_n=R,\n    R_i=R_(i+1)+h_i,\n    z_i=-z_(i+1)/d_i               if |z_(i+1)|<d_i,\n    z_i=z_(i+1)+h_i sign(z_(i+1))  if |z_(i+1)|>=d_i,\n    d_i=h_i-1.                                               (15)\n\nThe second case cannot have z_(i+1)=0. Its branch set A_i is both upper\ngroups in the first case, and the group of the sign of z_(i+1) in the\nsecond. Fractions are exactly 2r/N or r/N. For Law I this is exactly the\ntop-two-coordinate rule with a singleton fallback: the two-level gap in\nw' is (N/r)|z_(i+1)|, so the strict two-group guard is |z_(i+1)|<d_i.\n\nAll z_i, branch sets, thresholds, initial coordinates, and multiplicities\nare finite expressions determined from the full known word. Equation(15)\nis a mathematical definition, never an executed recurrence. If desired,\nwrite z_i=A_i^sc z_(i+1)+B_i^sc with A_i^sc=-1/d_i,B_i^sc=0 in the first\ncase, and A_i^sc=1,B_i^sc=h_i sign(z_(i+1)) in the second. Then\n\n    z_0=t product_(i<n) A_i^sc\n        +sum_(i<n) B_i^sc product_(j<i) A_j^sc.               (16)\n\nThis is not an average over incompatible itineraries. Once the finite\ndictionary is fixed, start the actual function (5) at\n\n    x_0=alpha(S+R)e+b z_0 u+Z.                              (17)\n\nEquivalently its coordinate levels are\n\n    w_(0,j)=q(S+R)/(2r) + N z_0/(2r), j in G_+,\n    w_(0,j)=q(S+R)/(2r) - N z_0/(2r), j in G_-,\n    w_(0,j)=-(S+R)+(N/sigma)Z_j, j in I.                    (18)\n\nTheir sum is zero. This specifies the full real initial vector and every\ncoordinate multiplicity, even when r or q is enormous.\n\n## 5. Entire actual run, thresholds, positive heights, and Gram\n\nAt every original i define x_i=alpha R_i e+b z_i u+Z. The terminal\ninequality(12) persists backward. In a two-group inverse, |z_i|<1 and\nR_i=R_(i+1)+h_i, so R_i-max(1,|z_i|) is strictly larger than the previous\nlower bound. In a one-group inverse, |z_i|=|z_(i+1)|+h_i>1, so\nR_i-|z_i|=R_(i+1)-|z_(i+1)| >= R_(i+1)-max(1,|z_(i+1)|).\nThus the same Z satisfies every original support and ordering condition.\n\nThe actual gradient and value at every query, terminal included, are\n\n    g_i=alpha e+b clip(z_i)u,\n    F(x_i)=alpha^2(R_i-1/2)+b^2 Hub(z_i),\n    Hub(s)=s^2/2 if |s|<=1, and |s|-1/2 otherwise,\n    delta_i=alpha^2(R_i-1)+b^2(|z_i|-1)_+>0.                 (19)\n\nOne can also see the strict correlated guards directly from (10). In the\ntwo-group case, with R'=R_(i+1), z'=z_(i+1),\n\n    theta_i=q(R'+d_i)/(2r),\n    max w'=[qR'+N|z'|]/(2r),\n    theta_i+d_i=[qR'+N d_i]/(2r).\n\nIn the one-group case,\n\n    theta_i=[qR'+N|z'|+2d_i(N-r)]/(2r),\n    theta_i+d_i=max w'+N d_i/r.\n\nAll theta_i>0, and the guards are strict. The first choice's guard is\nexactly the first condition in (15). Therefore (10) yields every displayed\nx_i and support. Alternatively, (19) gives the actual update\n\n    R_(i+1)=R_i-h_i,\n    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i clip(z_i), Z_(i+1)=Z_i,                  (20)\n\nwhich is verified by both cases of (15). Induction proves the WHOLE\nforward run on the unchanged function and every physical step. All large\nbridges and leaves in (2) remain present.\n\nFor c_i=clip(z_i), the full Gram of x_0 and every queried/terminal gradient\nis given explicitly by\n\n    <g_i,g_j>=alpha^2+b^2 c_i c_j,\n    <x_0,g_i>=alpha^2(S+R)+b^2 z_0 c_i,\n    ||x_0||^2=alpha^2(S+R)^2+b^2 z_0^2+||Z||^2.              (21)\n\nIt is the actual Euclidean Gram, hence positive semidefinite. Together\nwith (8), this proves every directed interpolation and optimum row; no\nindependent PSD surrogate or expected support was substituted.\n\n## 6. Explicit support reentry on the ENTIRE prescribed word\n\nThis is a parameter specialization of either law, not a third law. Take\nterminal t=a/2, R=2, Z=0, with any allowed r,q. Apply definition(15) to\nthe entire H_m, retaining all earlier indices. Its last five query scalars\ncan be written symbolically without replaying the word:\n\n    z_(n-1)=-1/2,\n    z_(n-2)=1/(2beta),\n    z_(n-3)=J:=1+a+1/(2beta),\n    z_(n-4)=-J/(beta c),\n    z_(n-5)=-J/(beta c)-(1+a).                              (22)\n\nThe excesses at these queries, in backward order, are a,beta,a,beta*c,a.\nThe first two choices are two-group choices since a/2<a and 1/2<beta.\nThe next is one-group since 1/(2beta)>1/2>a. Next J<beta*c:\nJ<3/2+500/999<21/10, while beta*c>(999/1000)(12/5)>23/10.\nFinally J/(beta*c)>a because a beta c=beta^2<1<J.\n\nConsequently the actual queried supports, chronologically, are\n\n    G_-, G_+ union G_-, G_+, G_+ union G_-, G_+ union G_-.\n\nThe minus group leaves and then re-enters; every height is strictly\npositive by (19). At the two-group queries z_i is nonzero and |z_i|<1,\nso the projected coordinates on G_+, G_-, and I have three distinct\nlevels. This is not a two-level initial-gradient assertion or a formal\nface expectation. Earlier queries are exactly the full finite law(15).\n\nFor this specialization the whole-word attained formula is\n\n    E=[3+kappa*a^2/4]/[(S+2)^2+kappa*z_0^2].                 (23)\n\nThe z_0 in (23) is the full-history quantity(16), not the five-step prefix\nor suffix. Formula(23) supplies no useful gain claim.\n\n## 7. Exact own radius and terminal formula across the whole history\n\nSince the complete minimum is {0}, put zeta=||Z||^2/alpha^2. The exact\nradius and payoff of each finite instance are\n\n    rho^2=alpha^2[(S+R)^2+kappa*z_0^2+zeta],\n    Phi(t)=2Hub(t),\n    E(F,h)=[2R-1+kappa*Phi(t)]/\n                  [(S+R)^2+kappa*z_0^2+zeta].                (24)\n\nIn the original w normalization the complete initial variance is\n\n    (1/N)sum_j w_(0,j)^2\n      =q/(2r)(S+R)^2+N/(2r)z_0^2+(N/sigma^2)||Z||^2.       (25)\n\nThis is an equality, not a first-fold estimate. It retains rare-coordinate\nnormal displacement, arbitrary finite dimension and every backward fold.\nNo terminal function completion is used. We have not transferred this\nradius to a different interpolating function or claimed a bound for all\ncompletions of the same prefix.\n\nFor L>0 replace F by L*F and use steps h_i/L. The entire run is unchanged,\nand (24) is the same normalized W=2R payoff.\n\n## 8. A full-hierarchy scalar interval lemma, proved within this draft\n\nLet T_h(s)=s-h clip(s). Write Psi_j for its chronological composition\nover EVERY entry of H_j, and M_j=1+S_j. The following precise assertions\nhold for the SAME enclosing m:\n\n    |s|<=M_j => |Psi_j(s)|<=1,\n    |s|>=M_j => Psi_j(s)=sign(s)(|s|-S_j).                  (26)\n\nAt equality the formulas agree. The exterior assertion follows directly:\nevery partial physical mass is at most S_j, so all intermediate points\nretain their sign and have magnitude at least1.\n\nFor the interior assertion use induction, starting with H_0 empty and\nM_0=1. Put b_j=1+d, d=a*c^j<M_j by (4), and consider\nH_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), whose M_(j+1)=2M_j+d. All maps are odd, so\ntake s>=0. If s<=M_j, the first child outputs magnitude at most1;\nthe bridge then has magnitude at most d<M_j. The second child outputs\nmagnitude at most1. If M_j<s<=M_(j+1), the first child gives y=s-S_j>1.\nThe bridge gives y-b_j, which lies between -d and M_j. The second child\nagain outputs magnitude at most1. This proves (26) for every j.\n\nThe lemma is only an interval assertion. It does NOT say\n|Psi_m(s)|<=|s|/sqrt(1+2S) inside that interval, and does not evaluate\nthe optimized scalar Huber profile. Replacing it by that stronger\npointwise claim would be a new, unpaid theorem.\n\n## 9. Whole Law I deficit, and the exact count-dependent screen\n\nAll statements in this section concern the ENTIRE parameter domains in\nSection4, on every m>=1536, not a finite-prefix improvement.\n\nFirst suppose |z_0|>S+1. By (26) the scalar trajectory stays saturated\nwith one sign. The whole gradient is the same c_+ or c_- at every query\nand at the terminal. Call it g. From (19),\n\n    2F(x_n)=2<x_0,g>-(1+2S)||g||^2\n            <=||x_0||^2/(1+2S),                            (27)\n\nbecause ||x_0-(1+2S)g||^2>=0. This already pays every component of x_0.\n\nOtherwise |z_0|<=S+1 and (26) gives |t|<=1. Equation(24) yields\n\n    E <= [2R-1+kappa]/(S+R)^2.                              (28)\n\nIf D_count:=2S+1-kappa>0, put U=S+R>0. The identity\nU^2-D_count(2U-D_count)=(U-D_count)^2>=0 proves\n\n    E <= 1/(2S+1-kappa).                                   (29)\n\nTogether (27)--(29) give the same upper bound on the entire domain when\nD_count>0, since kappa>0. In Law I, kappa=N/(N-2)<=3. Thus\n\n    E <=1/(2S-2).                                          (30)\n\nBy accepted5fd396eba75651e7 at exactly equation(77),\n\n    B_n E < (237/250)(1+2S)/(2S-2) < 19/20.                (31)\n\nFor the last strict inequality, (237/250)*(475/474)=19/20, and\n(1+2S)/(2S-2)=1+3/(2S-2)<475/474 whenever S>712; here S>1536.\n\nThe Huber member is attained by t=0, R=S+1, Z=0, any r,q. Then z_i=0\nat every index, and (24) gives exactly1/(1+2S). Therefore for Law I\n\n    1/(1+2S) <= sup E <=1/(2S-2),\n    sup E / H_base <=1+3/(2S-2).                            (32)\n\nAll new claims in (26)--(32) are unverified. If correct, they exclude a\nnonvanishing useful gain of Law I on the required benchmark. They do not\nprove its exact optimum is Huber and do not exclude a tiny finite gain\nover K_n or F_ray_n. Since those channels are at least H_base, any gain\nover either is also at most the relative error in (32).\n\nFor Law II the exact sufficient screen is\n\n    kappa <= (1+2S)/475 => B_n E <19/20.                    (33)\n\nMore generally, whenever 0<kappa<1+2S,\n\n    B_n E < (237/250)/[1-kappa/(1+2S)].                    (34)\n\nThe dimension/cardinality condition is essential: kappa=N/q. There is no\nuniform <=3 bound after copying the two active groups. Taking q=1 and\narbitrary integer r gives kappa=2r+1. These are exact finite coordinates,\nnot negligible-probability formal states. Equation(25) still charges their\nreal radius. A small inactive fraction can defeat this screen, which is\nnot evidence that it produces a useful terminal score.\n\n## 10. What the thin-inactive-group law actually reduces to\n\nFor any fixed word h with all h_i>1 define the scalar symmetric Huber\nprofile, on the real line and with its own minimum0, by\n\n    V_Hub(h)=sup_(s!=0) Phi(Psi_h(s))/s^2,                   (35)\n\nwhere Psi_h is the actual chronological composition of T_(h_i). This is\none fixed convex C1 1-smooth function Hub, varying only its initial point.\nThe elementary constant-slope example s=1+2S gives V_Hub>=H_base.\nNo accepted fact has identified V_Hub with K_n or the alternating-ray\nchannel; no such identification is assumed here.\n\nFor an instance of either law with z_0!=0, remove the nonnegative zeta\nin the denominator of (24) to obtain the weighted-average bound\n\n    E <= [ (S+R)^2 E_car(R) + kappa*z_0^2 E_sc ] /\n                       [ (S+R)^2 + kappa*z_0^2 ],\n    E_car(R)=(2R-1)/(S+R)^2 <=1/(1+2S),\n    E_sc=Phi(t)/z_0^2 <=V_Hub(h).                            (36)\n\nThe carrier bound follows from\n(S+R)^2-(1+2S)(2R-1)=(R-S-1)^2. If z_0=0, then t=0 and the\nsame upper bound follows directly. Consequently every Law II instance\nsatisfies E<=V_Hub(h), for every word in its h_i>1 domain.\n\nThe converse SUPREMUM relation needs attention to inverse branches and\nstrict guards. For one scalar step h=1+d, the smallest possible magnitude\nof an input with output magnitude u>=0 is\n\n    rho_d(u)=u/d if 0<=u<=d, and u+h if u>d.                 (37)\n\nTo prove this, inputs of magnitude at most1 give output magnitude d times\ntheir magnitude. For inputs of magnitude r>=1, the output magnitude is\n|r-h|. When u<=d the outer inverse h-u is at least1 and the core inverse\nu/d is at most1. When u>d, the only admissible outer inverse is h+u.\nAll alternatives are included; oddness removes the sign ambiguity.\n\nThe function rho_d is increasing and left-continuous. Therefore composing\n(37) backward minimizes the initial magnitude among ALL scalar trajectories\nhaving a given terminal magnitude. Indeed a larger intermediate magnitude\ncannot lower the earlier minimum, by monotonicity at each preceding step.\nDenote the resulting finite composition by r_h(u).\n\nOur strict inverse (15) agrees with these minimal magnitudes except when an\nintermediate u equals d. Those equality choices are not silently admitted:\n(15) uses its outer branch there so that every declared support is strict.\nHowever (37) and its finite composition are left-continuous and strictly\nincreasing. For any terminal u>0, choose positive u_l increasing to u and\navoiding the finitely many threshold preimages of this finite piecewise\naffine composition. The strict construction then has\n\n    |z_0(u_l)|=r_h(u_l) ->r_h(u), Phi(u_l)->Phi(u).            (38)\n\nOnly finite real instances are used at each l. No trajectory limit is\noffered as an attained witness. Thus the supremum of E_sc over the strict\nlaw is exactly V_Hub(h): replacing any scalar prehistory by the minimal\none cannot reduce Phi(u)/initial^2, and (38) approximates boundary cases.\n\nNow choose such a strict scalar history, any fixed R>max(1,u), and Z=0.\nWith q=1 and r arbitrarily large, kappa=2r+1 tends to infinity and (24)\ntends to its E_sc. Together with (36), this proves, as one joint draft,\n\n    sup_(ALL finite Law II instances) E(F,h)=V_Hub(h).       (39)\n\nThis is an equality of suprema. It does not assert finite attainment when\nthe carrier term is strictly below E_sc. The required finite-real control\nis explicit: if E_sc>eta>E_car(R), any allowed finite kappa satisfying\n\n    kappa > (S+R)^2[eta-E_car(R)] /\n                         [z_0^2(E_sc-eta)]                 (40)\n\ngives E>eta. This still pays the actual radius(24). Formula(40) is\nconditional on a scalar history with that score; none beating the target\nis supplied in this invocation.\n\n## 11. Remaining scalar interface and two unsuccessful direct attempts\n\nFor the hierarchy, (26) gives an exact division of (35):\n\n    V_Hub(H_m)=max{1/(1+2S), L_m^2},\n    L_m=sup_(0<|s|<=S+1) |Psi_m(s)|/|s|.                   (41)\n\nThe exterior supremum is 1/(1+2S), attained at |s|=1+2S, by direct\noptimization of [2(|s|-S)-1]/s^2. On the interior the terminal is in\n[-1,1], so its value is the square appearing in (41). Thus Law II's\nremaining issue is a concrete scalar amplitude profile, not a free\nmultidimensional covariance or an uncharged coordinate multiplicity.\n\nDirect attempt 1 was to use interval absorption(26) alone to bound (41).\nIt gives |Psi_m(s)|<=1 but cannot bound the ratio for small or intermediate\n|s| with the required 1/sqrt(B_n) constant. This is precisely the term\nwhich survives when kappa grows. Treating the bound1 as if it were a\nLipschitz constant of order1/sqrt(S) would be invalid.\n\nDirect attempt 2 was to propagate the interior ratio through the two\nidentical children using independent child suprema. This gives only a\nsufficient bound and may combine incompatible maximizing inputs. The\nactual second child receives T_(1+a*c^j)(Psi_j(s)); it cannot choose its\nown maximizing argument. Conversely, the exact inverse (37) is a finite\ndescription but its chronological, threshold-conditioned extremum has\nnot been evaluated here. Neither restating that composition nor assuming\nits critical inputs align establishes an attained gain or a deficit.\n\nThe construct-counterexamples audit refutes the tempting extension of\nthe Law I carrier bound: q=1,r>1 gives kappa>3, and r can grow with S.\nAll strict guards and the finite real radius remain valid. This is a\ncounterexample to that proposed proof step, not a counterexample to the\nminimax conjecture or to V_Hub<=K_n. No counterexample to the latter\ninequality was found or asserted.\n\nTo obtain a useful Law II result one still must either produce an exact\nscalar history with quantitative Phi(Psi_m(s))/s^2 beyond the COMPLETE\nK/ray channel and toward1/B_n, or prove the relevant uniform upper bound\nfor (41). To leave the scalar reduction, a genuinely new correlated rule\nmust change the persistent upper-group identities, split their equality\nclasses, use unequal group dynamics, or otherwise break the single fixed\nface's affine-plus-Huber decomposition. These are interfaces, not extra\nlaws attempted in this invocation. Arbitrary correlated simplex starts\nand the separate every-n/every-h target remain open.\n\n## 12. Claimed scoped conclusion and the unchanged benchmark\n\nFor every integer m>=1536 fix the SAME enclosing parameters\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)),\n    a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j, b_j=1+d_j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j, n=2^m-1,\n    H_base=1/(1+2S_m).                                      (1.1)\n\nAll children use this enclosing m. Let alpha_plus,alpha_minus belong to\n[0,1], with arbitrary dependence on m and the complete word, and put\n\n    f(x)=alpha_plus*x^2/2  (x>=0),\n         alpha_minus*x^2/2 (x<=0).                         (1.2)\n\nFor any initial point of positive closest distance to argmin f, use the\nORIGINAL physical updates x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t*f'(x_t), with h=H_m, and set\n\n    V=2f(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2.                         (1.3)\n\nThe joint argument below claims, uniformly over both curvatures and both\nlegitimate initial signs,\n\n    V <= H_base/2,                 m>=1536.                (1.4)\n\nThe constants and threshold1536 are independent of the curvature choices.\nThe same bound therefore holds for their supremum. This is a family upper\nbound, NOT an upper bound for the unrestricted W_n(H_m).\n\nKeep the exact original benchmark definitions\n\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_N=max_(0<=j<N) Omega(C_j,C_(N-j-1)),\n    B_N=max_(0<=j<N) G(C_j,B_(N-j-1)), U_N^ZJ=1/B_N.        (1.5)\n\nThese retain every integer split and are only definitions, not executed\nrecurrences. Accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7, (36)--(38),(77), supplies\n\n    2<c<121/50, 1/4<a<21/50,\n    M_j=v*c^j+(1-v)*2^j<=c^j, v=a/(c-2) in(0,1),\n    B_n*H_base<237/250.                                    (1.6)\n\nOnly these hierarchy/benchmark parts of that fact are used. No spiral\nconstruction conclusion is imported. Consequently (1.4), if verified, gives\n\n    B_n*V <= B_n*H_base/2 <237/500.                         (1.7)\n\nAccepted b98bb807d514e80d and1fca3e5dd555cb4a give\n\n    H_base <= K_n(H_m) <= F_ray_n(H_m) <= W_n(H_m).          (1.8)\n\nHere K_n is the maximum of the global Huber term and EVERY single-cut term;\nF_ray_n is the full feasible alternating-ray maximum, not a chosen cut.\nThus adjoining this entire two-curvature family to either complete channel\ndoes not improve that channel on any of these horizons. No new formula for\nthe full ray maximum is asserted or needed.\n\nThe original question asks about all sufficiently large horizons and every\nnonnegative word, or a full-class worst-case upper certificate for a negative\nanswer. Neither conclusion follows from (1.4). We keep W=2R throughout.\n\n## 2. One global function, all minima, actual normalization and all rows\n\nThe single derivative is alpha_plus*x on the positive ray and alpha_minus*x\non the negative ray, with value0 at0. It is continuous and nondecreasing.\nOn each ray it is 1-Lipschitz. Across0, for u,v>=0,\n\n    |f'(u)-f'(-v)|=alpha_plus*u+alpha_minus*v<=u+v.\n\nHence (1.2) is globally convex, C1 and 1-smooth. Its exact minimum is0.\nIf both curvatures are positive its complete minimum set is{0}. If just one\ncurvature vanishes, its minimum set is the whole closed ray of zero curvature.\nIf both vanish it is all of R and there is no initial point of positive\nclosest distance. This last function contributes no admissible instance.\n\nWhen the minimum set is a ray, a legitimate initial point lies strictly on\nthe other ray, its closest minimizer is0, and its distance is |x_0|. A later\nentry into the flat ray is a minimizer and all subsequent updates are fixed.\nStarting on the flat ray is excluded from (1.3); it is not assigned a fictitious\npositive closest radius. For positive initial distance, positive homogeneity\nallows x_0=+1 or-1 without changing (1.3). Its true distance is then1.\n\nAdvertised smoothness is1 regardless of max(alpha_plus,alpha_minus).\nFor advertised L>0 use L*f and h_t/L; this produces exactly the same points\nand payoff. No smaller actual smoothness is substituted while holding h fixed.\n\nAll physical steps, for an arbitrary nonnegative finite word, are exactly\n\n    x_(t+1)=(1-h_t*alpha_sign(x_t))*x_t, x_t!=0;\n    x_(t+1)=0, x_t=0.                                     (2.1)\n\nThe multiplier is positive/preserving for h_t*alpha<1, zero/absorbing at\nequality, and negative/crossing for h_t*alpha>1. A zero step is the identity;\nzero curvature is the identity on its entire ray; arbitrary large or unordered\nsteps require no exception. Repeated queries always use exactly the same\nderivative and objective. Knot0 and both slopes were fixed before the run.\nThe integral on a ray to radius u is exactly alpha*u^2/2; no hidden area is\ndiscarded. No spline completion or radius-changing modification is used.\n\nFor every actual sample, including the unqueried terminal and every minimizer,\nwrite g_i=f'(x_i), f_i=f(x_i). Use the convention of fact1252b8823bb5a290:\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2.              (2.2)\n\nFor two points on the same ray of curvature A,\n\n    Q_ij=-A*(1-A)*(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0.                       (2.3)\n\nFor opposite rays, let x_i=sigma*u, x_j=-sigma*v, u,v>=0,\nand let A,B be their respective fixed curvatures. Expansion gives\n\n    Q_ij=-A*(1-A)*u^2/2-B*(1-B)*v^2/2-A*(1-B)*u*v<=0.\n                                                                    (2.4)\n\nReversing i,j replaces the last coefficient by B*(1-A), again nonnegative\nbefore its minus sign. These formulas include all remote pairs, both directed\noptimum rows, ties, zero curvatures and arbitrary minimizers on a flat ray.\nThe actual full Gram matrix is z*z^T for z=(x_0,g_0,...,g_n), so is PSD.\nThus this is an explicit globally interpolating function, not merely a list\nof local updates whose remote rows are left unchecked.\n\n## 3. Exact two-state composition, including equalities\n\nReflect the line if necessary and label the two rays F and S by their\ncurvatures p>=q>=0. F denotes the larger-curvature ray; labels are arbitrary\nwhen p=q. For a nonzero state its data are(sign label, positive magnitude).\nAdd an absorbing zero state. At level j define T_j by\n\n    (u,A) -> (u,(1-u*b_j)A) if u*b_j<1;\n    (u,A) -> zero             if u*b_j=1;\n    (u,A) -> (other,(u*b_j-1)A) if u*b_j>1,               (3.1)\n\nwhere u is p or q according to the current ray; the ray label is retained\nseparately even when p=q. Zero maps to zero. If Map_j is the complete\nstate-and-magnitude map for H_j, then\n\n    Map_0=identity, Map_(j+1)=Map_j composed with T_j composed with Map_j. (3.2)\n\nThe rightmost map acts first. This exactly composes every original physical\nstep; it does not replace a block by its mass. In particular H_m ends with\nthe original level0 leaf b_0=1+a. The same formula (3.1) at that leaf applies\nto its ACTUAL preterminal sign, including equality and an already zero state.\n\nWe now replace this recursion by closed products. Define\n\n    P_j(t)=product_(ell=0)^(j-1)\n                      (1-t*b_ell)^(2^(j-ell-1)), P_0(t)=1,\n    r=min({j in[0,m-1]:p*b_j>=1} union{m}),\n    s=min({j in[0,m-1]:q*b_j>=1} union{m}).               (3.3)\n\nThese are weak crossing thresholds: equality is explicitly included.\nSince b_j strictly increases, r<=s. All factors in P_r(p) and P_s(q)\nare strictly positive. For any curvature u whose first weak threshold is r<m put\n\n    eta_r(u)=P_r(u)*(u*b_r-1)>=0.                         (3.4)\n\nFor a unit initial radius, A_m(F),A_m(S) below are the terminal magnitudes.\nThe payoff is its squared magnitude times the terminal curvature.\n\nCASE I: r=s=m. There are no crossings or threshold hits at any query:\n\n    A_m(F)=P_m(p), A_m(S)=P_m(q),\n    V_F=p*P_m(p)^2, V_S=q*P_m(q)^2.                     (3.5)\n\nExclude the flat initial sign when its curvature is0, as in Section2.\n\nCASE II: r=s<m. For either starting ray with curvature u, let v be the other\ncurvature, and put N=2^(m-r-1). Then\n\n    A_m(u)=[eta_r(u)*P_r(v)]^N\n                   *product_(j=r+1)^(m-1)(v*b_j-1)^(2^(m-j-1)),\n    V_u=v*A_m(u)^2.                                     (3.6)\n\nA nonzero terminal is on the other ray. A zero expression denotes absorption,\nnot a prescribed nonzero sign. Thus (3.6) remains exact if one curvature is\nexactly1/b_r and the other is larger: it does NOT incorrectly declare both\nstarting signs killed. Only the starting ray whose eta factor is zero dies.\n\nTo prove (3.6), all H_r subwords preserve each sign and multiply it by P_r\nat that curvature. Contract the notation to the ORIGINAL H_r subwords and\nthe intervening checkpoints of levels>=r. There are2^(m-r) such subwords\nand2^(m-r)-1 checkpoints. Provided the trajectory has not hit zero, the\ncheckpoints all cross. The level-r checkpoints occupy even zero-based\ncheckpoint indices, hence all take the starting curvature u. Every higher\ncheckpoint occupies an odd index and takes v. Half the H_r subwords take\neach curvature. A level j occurs2^(m-j-1) times. Multiplying precisely these\nfactors yields (3.6). If an equality is encountered, its zero factor gives\nthe same absorbed result; alternatively continuity gives that endpoint.\n\nCASE III: r<s. Define the strictly positive denominator and fixed ratio\n\n    D=P_r(q)*(1-q*b_r), R=eta_r(p)/D>=0,\n    K=0 if s=m, and K=2^(m-s)-1 if s<m.                  (3.7)\n\nThen the exact full terminal formulas are\n\n    A_m(S)=|P_m(q)|*R^K,\n    A_m(F)=|P_m(q)|*R^(K+1),\n    V_S=q*P_m(q)^2*R^(2K),\n    V_F=q*P_m(q)^2*R^(2K+2).                            (3.8)\n\nUse R^0=1, also when R=0. Nonzero terminals are on the q ray. If q=0,\nthe F-start formula can give positive terminal magnitude on the flat ray,\nbut its objective is exactly0. This does not turn it into a non-minimizer.\n\nFor a proof, H_(r+1) sends every surviving starting sign to S, with gains\n\n    A_(r+1)(S)=P_r(q)^2*(1-q*b_r),\n    A_(r+1)(F)=eta_r(p)*P_r(q)=R*A_(r+1)(S).             (3.9)\n\nThe report-local R in (3.7) is a gain ratio, distinct from the original\nperformance normalization R=W/2. If Map_j has the constant surviving output\nand gain ratio R, applying (3.2)\npreserves the same gain ratio. Its S-input gain is squared and multiplied\nby |1-q*b_j| if that checkpoint preserves the q sign, and by\nR*|1-q*b_j| if it crosses. At equality the whole map is zero. Up to the\nfirst q threshold, its S gain is P_j(q). For all later levels, multiplication\nof the factors R with their exact tree multiplicities gives total exponent\nsum_(j=s)^(m-1)2^(m-j-1)=2^(m-s)-1. This proves (3.8).\nAt an equality q*b_s=1, its P_m(q) factor vanishes, so both starts die.\nIf p*b_r=1, R=0: the F start dies immediately, and the S start dies on its\nfirst excursion to F if one occurs. These cases agree with (3.1).\n\nEquations (3.5),(3.6),(3.8) are exact finite terminal products for ALL curvature\npairs and ALL m in (1.1), not just fixed-parameter limits or a numerical run.\nThe state map (3.1) also gives every intermediate query and objective:\nx_t=sign_t*A_t, g_t=alpha_sign_t*x_t, f_t=alpha_sign_t*A_t^2/2.\nThus it fixes the full actual history and Gram as well as its terminal.\n\nFor clarity about the final leaf, if Case II has r>=1, that leaf preserves\nthe terminal ray v and is already counted inside P_r(v). If r=0 it crosses\nfrom the initial curvature u to v and is included among the level0 factors.\nIn Case III with r>=1 the final leaf preserves S. If r=0, s>=2 it also\npreserves S; if r=0,s=1 a surviving penultimate level1 checkpoint crosses\nS to F, and the final leaf crosses F to S. Equality at that penultimate\ncheckpoint kills the state. All these are physical updates, not truncations\nbefore the leaf. Zero states stay zero in every case.\n\n## 4. Uniform elementary estimates used in the joint proof\n\nWe derive every additional bound here rather than importing an unverified\ndraft. From m>=1536, beta>99/100 and (1.6),\n\n    2/5<a<21/50<1/2, 2<c<5/2, a*c=beta<1,\n    c*(c-1)<(121/50)*(71/50)<7/2.                       (4.1)\n\nFor example a>(99/100)/(121/50)=99/242>2/5. Also S_1=1+a>d_1=beta,\nand the original recurrence gives\n\n    S_(j+1)-d_(j+1)=2(S_j-d_j)+1+(3-c)*d_j>0.\n\nConsequently S_j>d_j for j>=1. Since m>=3, S_(m-1)>3 and\n\n    1+2S_m<2c^m,\n    1+2S_m=4S_(m-1)+2b_(m-1)+1<7S_(m-1).              (4.2)\n\nThe first inequality uses M_m<=c^m; the second uses b_(m-1)<S_(m-1)+1.\n\nFor every j>=2 we will use\n\n    S_j/b_j>7/5,             (S_j-b_0)/b_j>9/10.        (4.3)\n\nAt j=2, S_2=3+2a+beta>479/100 and b_2=1+beta*c<171/50, so\nS_2/b_2>479/342>7/5 (2395>2394). Also\nS_2-b_0=2+a+beta>339/100, so (S_2-b_0)/b_2>339/342>9/10.\nFor induction b_(j+1)=c*b_j-(c-1)<c*b_j. If S_j>t*b_j then\nS_(j+1)>(2t+1)b_j>t*c*b_j for t=7/5, since c<5/2<2+1/t.\nIf S_j-b_0>t*b_j then S_(j+1)-b_0>(2t+1)b_j+b_0>t*c*b_j\nfor t=9/10 by the same comparison. This proves both bounds for every j>=2;\nno false assertion of monotonicity of S_j/b_j is needed.\n\nIf r>=1 and u has first weak threshold r, then\nu*b_(r-1)<1<=u*b_r and\n\n    0<=eta_r(u)\n       <=(1-u*b_(r-1))*(u*b_r-1)\n       <=(b_r-b_(r-1))^2/(4*b_r*b_(r-1))\n       <(c-1)^2/(4c)<1/4.                              (4.4)\n\nIndeed all omitted factors of P_r(u) lie in(0,1]; the middle quadratic\nhas the displayed maximum by completing its square. With t=b_r/b_(r-1),\n1<t<c and (t-1)^2/t=t-2+1/t increases for t>1. Finally (c-1)^2<c for\n2<c<5/2. If r=0, eta_0(u)=u*b_0-1<=a. Hence in ALL cases\n\n    eta_r(u)<=a; for r>=1 it is <1/4.                   (4.5)\n\nFor any pre-threshold scalar block, all its factors belong to(0,1], and\n1-z<=exp(-z) for z>=0 gives\n\n    P_j(t)<=exp(-t*S_j).                               (4.6)\n\nWhenever r<s, P_s(q)/D is a product with nonnegative integer exponents\nof pre-threshold factors, so it is at most1. It retains a factor\n1-q*b_(s-1) when s>=r+2. When s=r+1 it equals P_r(q).\nDeleting the factors of D deletes exactly the mass S_r+b_r from P_s.\nIn particular for r=0 it gives\n\n    P_s(q)/D<=exp(-q*(S_s-b_0)).                        (4.7)\n\nFor later use, exp(7/5)>4: its Taylor terms of orders0 through5 already\nsum to more than4. Also exp(9/10)>2 by its first three terms, and e>5/2\nby its first four terms. These are inequalities between exact series terms,\nnot floating-point estimates or evaluated mathematical programs.\n\n## 5. Product envelope for all high-level factors\n\nThis is an algebraic estimate inside the same joint proof. Suppose a\nnonnegative sequence of complete block gains has initial value J at level t\nand for subsequent levels is squared and multiplied by factors at most\nz*c^ell, ell=0,...,k-1.
