Original target OPEN.", "evidence": "# Rotating three-group simplex faces with the entire carrier price\n\n**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED PROOF ATTEMPT.** Worker max2, project\ngd-exact-profile-20260926, allocated invocation after ended69, ceiling70 /\nattempted71. The formal gate is closed. None of the new mathematics below is\na fact, and none is used as an independently accepted intermediate premise.\nThere is one substantive mechanism: a maximal-support inverse on a genuine\ntwo-dimensional triangular face. No second mechanism or renewal is opened.\n\nThe construction has three participating upper groups and changing support\nidentities. In the final five original queries of the diagnostic word its\nsupports include the two different edges {1,3} and {1,2}, separated by the\nfull triangle. Three actual gradients are noncollinear. Thus this is not the\nprevious fixed face barycenter plus one scalar Huber direction.\n\nThe new whole-law conclusion is a **scoped obstruction**, not a gain witness.\nFor all parameters in the law defined below, on every SAME-enclosing-m\nH_(m,1/4), m>=1536, its actual normalized terminal score E obeys\n\n    E <= 1/(D_m-8),       B_(2^m-1) E < 19/20.               (A)\n\nThe family contains the attained score 1/D_m. Consequently its entire\nsupremum is between 1/D_m and 1/(D_m-8), and any relative improvement over\nthe COMPLETE K/ray lower bounds is at most D_m/(D_m-8). That factor tends\nto one. No exact domination E<=K_n or E<=F_ray_n is claimed.\n\nThe scope is explicit: three equal-sized upper groups of size r>=1, q>=r\nremaining coordinates, and terminal transverse point anywhere in the\nclosed centered triangle. The common-direction price is included in the\nsame static function's OWN radius. Small inactive fractions q<r, arbitrary\nterminal transverse points outside that triangle, arbitrary simplex starts,\nand arbitrary terminal completions are not ruled out.\n\n## 1. Original target and accepted inputs\n\nFor every integer n>=0, L>0, and predetermined finite nonnegative word\nh=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), the original error is\n\n    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2),\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i).\n\nThe supremum is over every finite Euclidean dimension, every convex\ndifferentiable function with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimizer\nx_star, and every x_0!=x_star. Thus W=2R in the project's normalization.\nThe problem asks whether W_n^star=inf_(h>=0) W_n(h) satisfies\n\n    lim_(n->infinity) B_n W_n^star=1.\n\nAn affirmative answer requires: for every epsilon>0 there is a finite\nN_epsilon such that for EVERY n>=N_epsilon and EVERY nonnegative word h of\nlength n, W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n. The same N_epsilon must work for all h.\nA negative answer requires a genuine upper bound on the full function-class\nsupremum for an infinite sequence of actual words. This report does neither.\nThe complete original PROBLEM.md is preserved without modification.\n\nRetain every original integer split:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    Gbench(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) Gbench(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                             (1)\n\nThe following accepted facts are used only at the stated scopes:\n\n* **931b9653ce0f7201**, read in full: C_n<=(n+1)^rho and\n  B_n<=(12/5)(n+1)^rho for every n with all splits, and B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50\n  for the exact hierarchy below, every m>=1536. Its full-W implication is\n  conditional. No full-function upper bound from that fact is assumed.\n* **b98bb807d514e80d**, read in full: the complete K_n includes 1/(1+2S),\n  where S=sum h. Its full definition is the maximum of that term and all\n  (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)].\n* **1fca3e5dd555cb4a**, statement read: K_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n, where F_ray_n\n  retains all feasible alternating-ray checkpoint choices and their remote\n  offsets and areas. No evaluation of F_ray_n on this hierarchy is used.\n* **1252b8823bb5a290**, statement and interpolation-necessity proof read:\n  the original ordered-row convention. All rows needed for the present\n  static function are also established directly below; its terminal is not\n  replaced by a maximal completion.\n\nThe previous reports 2993fddcc4a03577 and f7f9930b98aa7caa are awareness\nonly. Their inverse, metric, and obstruction claims are not premises.\n\nFix an arbitrary integer m>=1536 and use this SAME m at every child:\n\n    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum h, D_m=1+2S.                       (2)\n\nEvery actual h_i>1; no original leaf, bridge, or terminal is discarded.\nThe finite law below is defined more generally for every finite word with\nh_i>1, including the empty word. It does not purport to cover arbitrary\nnonnegative words. The original every-n/every-h question remains separate.\n\n## 2. Fixed real slopes, function, and complete minimum set\n\nChoose integers r>=1 and q>=r; both may depend on the whole word. Put\nN=3r+q. Partition the original N coordinates into three groups G_1,G_2,G_3\nof size r and a remaining set I of size q. Choose any real sigma>0. Write\nu_N=(1/N,...,1/N) and use the Euclidean subspace\n\n    E_N={x in R^N: sum_l x_l=0}.\n\nThere are exactly N fixed real slopes, all offsets zero:\n\n    s_l=sigma(e_l-u_N), 1<=l<=N,\n    D=conv{s_l:1<=l<=N},\n    F(x)=max_(g in D) [<x,g>-||g||^2/2], x in E_N.           (3)\n\nTheir exact Gram matrix is\n\n    <s_l,s_k>=sigma^2(1_(l=k)-1/N).                          (4)\n\nThe function does not change during the run. The maximization in (3) is\nover the entire convex hull, not merely over the original vertices. The\noriginal affine dictionary is max_l <s_l,x>, with all its offsets zero;\n(3) is the fixed quadratic smoothing used throughout this report.\n\nExistence of a maximizing g follows from compactness of D; strict concavity\nof the quadratic gives uniqueness. Completing the square identifies g as\nthe Euclidean projection of x onto D. Its exact condition is\n\n    <x-g,v-g><=0 for every v in D.                            (5)\n\nFor two inputs x,x', sum the two corresponding inequalities to obtain\n<x-x',g-g'> >= ||g-g'||^2. Hence ||g-g'||<=||x-x'||. Evaluating (3) at\neach other's maximizers gives a nonnegative first-order remainder bounded\nabove by <x'-x,g'-g>, hence by ||x'-x||^2. Therefore F is differentiable,\nconvex, finite everywhere, and grad F=g is 1-Lipschitz. Convexity also\nfollows directly by taking the supremum of the affine functions in (3).\n\nZero is in the relative interior of D in E_N. Indeed a centered vector w\nwith every w_l> -sigma/N belongs to D because w/sigma+u_N has positive\nentries summing to one. Thus for any x!=0, a sufficiently small t in (0,1)\nsatisfies tx in D and\n\n    F(x)>=(t-t^2/2)||x||^2>0.\n\nSince F(0)=0, the COMPLETE minimum set is exactly {0}. The smoothness\nconstant is exactly 1, since grad F(x)=x in a neighborhood of zero.\nSet f=L F for general L>0. The original GD update then uses grad F and\nthe same original h. The OWN closest-minimum radius is ||x_0||.\n\nThe subspace E_N is an ordinary Euclidean space of dimension N-1, not a\nvariable metric. An explicit orthonormal basis is\n(e_1+...+e_k-k e_(k+1))/sqrt(k(k+1)), 1<=k<N; its coordinates identify\n(3) with a function on R^(N-1) without altering any inner product.\n\n## 3. The two-dimensional transverse triangle and exact metric\n\nLet U=G_1 union G_2 union G_3 and define the actual face barycenter\n\n    c_face=sigma(1_U/(3r)-u_N),\n    alpha^2=||c_face||^2=sigma^2 q/(3rN), b=sigma/sqrt(r).\n\nDefine T={z in R^3:z_1+z_2+z_3=0} and its centered triangle\n\n    K=conv{e_j-(1,1,1)/3:1<=j<=3}\n     ={z in T:z_j>=-1/3 for every j}.\n\nThe isometry J:T->E_N puts z_j/sqrt(r) on every coordinate of G_j and\nzero on I. It is orthogonal to c_face. Thus\n\n    ||R c_face+b Jz||^2=alpha^2 R^2+b^2||z||^2,\n    kappa=b^2/alpha^2=3N/q<=12.                              (6)\n\nThe last inequality uses exactly the law's count condition q>=r. It is not\nan estimate valid for all possible group counts.\n\nFor z in T, let tau be the unique real number satisfying\n\n    sum_(j=1)^3 (z_j+1/3-tau)_+=1.\n\nSet p_j=(z_j+1/3-tau)_+ and v=p-(1,1,1)/3. At tau=0 the left side\nis at least sum_j(z_j+1/3)=1, so tau>=0. The residual z-v has value tau\non every positive-p coordinate and at most tau on every zero-p coordinate,\nand its sum is zero. Therefore <z-v,w-v><=0 for every w in K. This proves\nv=P_K(z), including all boundary cases, without a projection black box.\nDefine\n\n    Phi(z)=<z,v>-||v||^2/2.\n\nThe same calculation as (3)-(5) makes Phi a convex 1-smooth function with\ngradient v. The residual identities give\n\n    Phi(z)-||v||^2/2=<z-v,v>=tau.                            (7)\n\nFor z in K, v=z, tau=0 and 2Phi(z)=||z||^2<=2/3. The last bound follows\nby convexity of squared norm and the squared norm 2/3 of each vertex.\n\nFor ANY R>=1 and ANY z in T, the full ambient simplex projection at\n\n    x=R c_face+b Jz\n\nis exactly\n\n    g=c_face+b Jv.                                           (8)\n\nTo check against every original vertex, the active upper residual level is\n\n    sigma[(R-1)q/(3rN)+tau/r],\n\nand the residual on I is -sigma(R-1)/N. Their difference is\nsigma[(R-1)/3+tau]/r>=0. Other upper residuals are no larger. Thus (5)\nholds for all N original vertices, including those declared inactive. If\nR>1 the residual inequality for I is strict. At R=1 equality is harmless:\nthe projection is still unique, and every coordinate in I still has zero\nbarycentric weight.\n\nThe full function value and height are therefore\n\n    F(x)=alpha^2(R-1/2)+b^2 Phi(z),\n    F(x)-||g||^2/2=alpha^2(R-1)+b^2 tau.                     (9)\n\nThis is a genuine two-dimensional triangle process plus the actual common\ndirection. It is not the earlier affine-plus-scalar-Huber decomposition.\n\n## 4. A concrete finite inverse rule, with every threshold guard\n\nFix the entire finite word h with h_i>1 before choosing the instance.\nChoose a terminal z_n anywhere in K and R>=1. Ties between equal coordinate\nvalues below are broken by the fixed index order 1<2<3. No coordinates or\ngroup copies are created during the construction or the forward run.\n\nProceed symbolically backwards in the original chronological indices. For\none original step h=1+d, d>0, and a known centered future vector y, sort\nits coordinates y_(1)>=y_(2)>=y_(3), retaining their original identities.\nChoose the set A by exactly this rule:\n\n    if 3 y_(1)<d:             A={1,2,3};\n    else if y_(1)-y_(2)<d:    A={indices of y_(1),y_(2)};\n    otherwise:               A={index of y_(1)}.             (10)\n\nAll comparisons are exact real comparisons. Strict inequalities in the\nfirst two branches fix the rule at equality too; no limiting preimage is\nsilently admitted. Write k=|A| and set\n\n    tau=[sum_(j in A)y_j+d(1-k/3)]/k,\n    z_j=(h tau-y_j)/d           for j in A,\n    z_j=y_j-h/3                for j outside A.              (11)\n\nFor k=3, tau=0 and max y<d/3. For k=2, tau=(-y_(3)+d/3)/2>0 and\n\n    tau+d/3=(y_(1)+y_(2)+d)/2>y_(1).\n\nFor k=1, tau=y_(1)+2d/3>0 and tau+d/3=y_(1)+d>y_(1), since y_(1)>=0.\nConsequently in EVERY branch\n\n    y_j<tau+d/3 for every coordinate j.                      (12)\n\nFor j in A, (11) gives z_j+1/3-tau=(tau+d/3-y_j)/d>0.\nFor j outside A it gives z_j+1/3-tau=y_j-(tau+d/3)<0.\nThe active positive parts sum to\n\n    [k(tau+d/3)-sum_(j in A)y_j]/d=1.\n\nSumming (11) gives sum_j z_j=0. Hence tau really is the threshold of z,\nits exact active set is A, its projected gradient v has p_j>0 exactly on\nA, and direct substitution yields\n\n    z-h v=y.                                                 (13)\n\nThis proves ALL inverse guards, for every possible future vector, rather\nthan just the guards of the displayed short suffix. In particular it covers\nall previously visited and all subsequently specified supports when the\nfinite inverse dictionary is traversed forward.\n\nApply (10)-(11) at i=n-1,...,0, with y=z_(i+1), h=h_i. Denote the resulting\nthresholds and projected gradients by tau_i and v_i. At i=n take tau_n=0,\nv_n=z_n, and p_n=z_n+(1,1,1)/3. Every z_i is finite because the word is\nfinite, every h_i is finite, and every d_i>0. This is a textual finite\nselection rule; no recurrence or trajectory was executed by a program.\n\nAn explicit affine-product expression for every chosen initial coordinate\ncan also be given. Once A_i is fixed, let M_i be block diagonal with\nactive block -I/d_i+h_i 11^T/(k_i d_i), inactive identity block, and zero\ncross blocks. Let (b_i^aff)_j=h_i(1/k_i-1/3) on A_i and -h_i/3 elsewhere.\nThen\n\n    z_i=M_i z_(i+1)+b_i^aff,\n    z_0=(M_0...M_(n-1))z_n\n        +sum_(i=0)^(n-1)(M_0...M_(i-1))b_i^aff.              (14)\n\nAn empty product is identity. This is not a product of independently chosen\nbest histories: every A_i is selected from its one already fixed future.\nThe formula is part of the same joint derivation, not a computed artifact.\n\nEqual original coordinates cannot split in the forward run: the same\nthreshold map applies to each. Formula (13) also shows directly that any\nequality in a constructed predecessor is retained in its prescribed future.\nDistinct predecessors may merge. The fixed group sizes never change.\n\n## 5. Every original physical point, row, Gram entry, and radius\n\nPut S_i=sum_(t=i)^(n-1) h_t, with S_n=0, and R_i=R+S_i. The initial point\nand all its actual iterates are\n\n    x_i=R_i c_face+b Jz_i, 0<=i<=n,\n    g_i=c_face+b Jv_i,\n    F_i=alpha^2(R_i-1/2)+b^2 Phi(z_i).                        (15)\n\nIn literal original coordinates, for l in G_j and l in I respectively,\n\n    (x_i)_l=sigma[R_i q/(3rN)+(z_i)_j/r],\n    (x_i)_l=-sigma R_i/N;\n    (g_i)_l=sigma[(p_i)_j/r-1/N],\n    (g_i)_l=-sigma/N.                                        (16)\n\nThe ambient threshold applied to (x_i)_l/sigma+1/N is\n\n    theta_i=(R_i-1)q/(3rN)+tau_i/r.                          (17)\n\nIt gives positive-part barycentric entries (p_i)_j/r on G_j and zero on I.\nThe latter guard is exactly -[(R_i-1)/3+tau_i]/r<=0, strictly negative at\nevery queried i<n. The triangle active/inactive guards are (12). Thus the\nwhole known word fixes every original coordinate and every threshold.\n\nEquations (13) and R_i-h_i=R_(i+1) prove\nx_i-h_i g_i=x_(i+1) at each of the n original steps. At every queried index,\nR_i>1, so (9) gives strictly positive height. This includes all leaves and\nbridges, not just the five queries used to demonstrate rotation.\n\nFor the actual vectors, the complete Gram entries are\n\n    <x_i,x_j>=alpha^2 R_i R_j+b^2<z_i,z_j>,\n    <x_i,g_j>=alpha^2 R_i+b^2<z_i,v_j>,\n    <g_i,g_j>=alpha^2+b^2<v_i,v_j>.                           (18)\n\nIn particular (18) specifies the full original formal-vector Gram on\n(x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including the real terminal gradient. It is PSD because\nthe vectors in (16) are real vectors in one Euclidean space. No rank or\nsupport restriction is imposed on a claimed full PEP upper certificate.\n\nFor ANY ordered pair of samples i,j, expand (3) at their actual gradients:\n\n    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                              (19)\n\nThe inequality is the normal condition (5) for sample j, applied to g_i in\nD. Include the optimum sample x_star=g_star=F_star=0: then Q_(i,star)=0\nand Q_(star,i)=-[alpha^2(R_i-1)+b^2 tau_i]<=0. All remote, reversed,\nincoming and outgoing terminal rows are included. No prior row is replaced\nby an expected face or an averaged group history.\n\nBecause argmin F={0}, the OWN squared radius is exactly\n\n    R_init^2=||x_0||^2\n             =alpha^2(S+R)^2+b^2||z_0||^2>0.                (20)\n\nAt the terminal z_n in K, equations (9) and (20) give the quantitative exact\nterminal formula\n\n    E=2F(x_n)/R_init^2\n      =[2R-1+kappa||z_n||^2]\n        /[(S+R)^2+kappa||z_0||^2].                          (21)\n\nThis is the original W=2R score of f=L F about its unique true minimum.\nThe entire initial transverse history is present in the denominator. The\ncommon direction contributes alpha^2(S+R)^2; it has not been declared free.\n\n## 6. Actual rotation on the final five original queries\n\nFor every diagnostic m>=1536 choose the concrete terminal point\n\n    z_n=(a/4,0,-a/4).                                        (22)\n\nIt is strictly inside K. All r>=1, q>=r, sigma>0 and R>=1 are allowed;\nfor a single completely specified member one can take r=q=sigma=1 and\nR=S+1. Earlier coordinates are determined by (10)-(14), not by a reset.\n\nWe first justify the coarse inequalities used in this finite symbolic suffix\ncalculation. Since epsilon<=1/6144<1/1000, beta>999/1000. The elementary\nbounds 12/5<lambda<17/7 give c<(17/7)(1001/1000)<49/20. Therefore\n\n    2/5<a<5/12,  beta>999/1000, beta^2>19/20,\n    u:=1/(4beta)<13/50.                                      (23)\n\nFor the lower bound on a, beta/c>999/2450>2/5. The upper bound follows\nfrom beta<1, c>lambda>12/5. The bounds for beta^2 and u follow by squaring\nor multiplying positive rational quantities; they involve no numerical\nevaluation of an orbit.\n\nThe last five original d_i=h_i-1 values are\n\n    a, beta*c, a, beta, a,\n\nin forward order, because H_m ends in H_3 and a*c=beta. Put\nd_2=beta*c=beta^2/a. Starting from (22), the last two inverse steps use the\nfull-triangle branch and give\n\n    z_(n-1)=(-1/4,0,1/4),\n    z_(n-2)=(u,0,-u).                                        (24)\n\nTheir guards are 3a/4<a and 3/4<beta. At i=n-3, full support fails because\n3u>3/4>a, but the top-two gap is u<a by (23). Thus A_(n-3)={1,2}, and\n\n    tau=(u+a/3)/2, h=1+a,\n    z^E:=z_(n-3)=(h/6+u(a-1)/(2a),\n                         h/6+u(a+1)/(2a), -u-h/3),\n    v_(n-3)=(1/6-u/(2a), 1/6+u/(2a), -1/3).                (25)\n\nThe associated positive barycentric weights are\n1/2-u/(2a), 1/2+u/(2a), and zero. In particular both active weights are\nstrictly positive, and the edge is a real active face.\n\nThe entries of z^E have strict order z^E_2>z^E_1>z^E_3: the first\ndifference is u/a>0, while the second is\n\n    z^E_1-z^E_3=h/2+u(3a-1)/(2a)>0.\n\nAt i=n-4 the full-triangle inverse is valid. Indeed\n\n    3a z^E_2=(h/2)(3u+a)\n       < (17/24)(39/50+5/12)\n       =6103/7200<9/10<beta^2.\n\nThus 3 max z^E<d_2 and\n\n    z_(n-4)=-z^E/d_2.                                       (26)\n\nIts ordered coordinate identities are 3,1,2. For the next inverse at\ni=n-5, full support fails since\n\n    3(z_(n-4))_3=a(3u+h)/beta^2>a,\n\nusing 3u+h>1>beta^2. The top-two gap is\n\n    (z^E_1-z^E_3)/d_2\n      =[a h+u(3a-1)]/(2beta^2)<a.                            (27)\n\nFor the strict inequality, (23) and h<3/2 give\na(2beta^2-h)>(2/5)a>4/25, while\nu(3a-1)<(13/50)(1/4)=13/200<4/25. Thus A_(n-5)={3,1}.\n\nThe actual support sequence at those five original queries is therefore\n\n    {1,3}, {1,2,3}, {1,2}, {1,2,3}, {1,2,3}.                (28)\n\nThe terminal also has full support. Every group participates. In the\noriginal N-coordinate simplex, a displayed group in (28) means all its r\nactual copies are active, with the exact weight p_j/r from (16).\n\nThere is no scalar-line reduction: v_(n-2)=(u,0,-u) and\nv_(n-1)=(-1/4,0,1/4) are distinct points on the line with second coordinate\nzero, whereas the second coordinate of v_(n-3) in (25) is strictly positive.\nThese three transverse gradients are noncollinear. By the isometry J,\nthe same is true of the three ambient gradients after their common carrier\nis restored. No fixed vector plus one fixed scalar direction contains them.\n\nThis is a symbolic proof for every m>=1536 using the actual suffix of the\nsame enclosing word. It is not a computed five-step replay or a substitute\nfor the full-word accounting in (20)-(21).\n\n## 7. Whole-law comparison, including all earlier leaves and bridges\n\nThe proof of the obstruction requires no estimate that discards the initial\nhistory. Instead it uses the nonnegative term that history contributes to\nits OWN radius. For every allowed terminal and count choice put\n\n    eta=kappa||z_n||^2, 0<=eta<=12*(2/3)=8.\n\nEquation (21) gives\n\n    E <= (2R-1+eta)/(S+R)^2.\n\nFor D_word=1+2S>eta, the exact completed-square identity is\n\n    (S+R)^2-(D_word-eta)(2R-1+eta)\n       =[R-(S+1-eta)]^2>=0.\n\nThe numerator is positive because R>=1. Therefore\n\n    E <= 1/(D_word-eta) <= 1/(D_word-8) if D_word>8.          (29)\n\nThis is uniform over EVERY finite r,q with q>=r, every sigma>0, every R>=1,\nevery z_n in the closed K, every branch history selected by (10), and all\nthe resulting original initial points. Boundary terminals and exact inverse\nthreshold equalities are included. The bound is not merely local in m.\n\nFor z_n=0 the rule always selects all three coordinates, all z_i=0, and\nchoosing R=S+1 gives by (21) the attained score\n\n    E=1/D_word.                                              (30)\n\nFor the rotating members (22), the more specific terminal charge is\neta=kappa a^2/8<=3a^2/2<25/96. Hence they even obey\nE<=1/(D_word-3a^2/2), by the same proof whenever its denominator is\npositive. No estimate of z_0 is assumed here.\n\nOn (2), D_word=D_m and S>n=2^m-1>=m>=1536, so D_m>3073>760.\nThe accepted comparison in fact931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_n/D_m<47/50.\nApplying it in its correct direction\nto the positive UPPER bound (29),\n\n    B_n E < (47/50) D_m/(D_m-8) < 19/20.                    (31)\n\nThe last inequality is equivalent, with positive denominators, to D_m>760.\nAlso D_m>136 yields\n\n    E<=1/(D_m-8)<17/(16D_m).                                 (32)\n\nEquation (32) holds only for THIS constructed class. It does not meet the\nfull-function hypothesis of the conditional theorem in the accepted fact.\nIt cannot be substituted for W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m).\n\nBecause the COMPLETE K_n(H_m)>=1/D_m and F_ray_n(H_m)>=K_n(H_m), we also have\n\n    E/K_n(H_m)<=D_m/(D_m-8),\n    E/F_ray_n(H_m)<=D_m/(D_m-8).                             (33)\n\nBoth comparison denominators retain every original leaf, bridge, and\nfeasible checkpoint choice. Neither is replaced by a selected local cut.\nSince D_m>1+2(2^m-1) tends to infinity, the right sides tend to one uniformly\nover the whole law. Combining (29) and (30) further gives\n\n    1 <= D_m sup_(this law) E <= D_m/(D_m-8) -> 1.           (34)\n\nThus the new face rotation has been made legal and quantitatively priced,\nbut it cannot deliver a nonvanishing benchmark gain in this count and\nterminal domain. The actual optimum inside the narrow bracket is not\nevaluated. In particular (33) does not assert E<=K_n or E<=F_ray_n.\n\n## 8. Scope attacks and the remaining vector question\n\nThe direct-proving screen has established, inside this one unverified draft,\nthe legal real geometry, all guards, own radius, noncollinearity, exact\nterminal formula, and the uniform comparison for the declared law.\n\nThe counterexample screen found two reasons not to overextend it:\n\n1. The metric bound kappa<=12 genuinely uses q>=r. For the algebraically\n   same face geometry with q=1 and arbitrary integer r, the exact metric\n   ratio is kappa=3(3r+1), which is unbounded. For a terminal triangle\n   vertex, eta=(2/3)kappa=2(3r+1), also unbounded. This refutes extending\n   the particular estimate eta<=8 to all copied three-group counts. It\n   does NOT refute a performance bound there, because the initial\n   transverse charge kappa||z_0||^2 remains to be paid. No second law is\n   claimed analyzed on that larger domain.\n2. The terminal restriction z_n in K is also load-bearing. For a general\n   transverse terminal z, formula (9) has 2Phi(z), not ||z||^2, and\n   2Phi(z) is unbounded along an outward vertex ray: for\n   w=e_1-(1,1,1)/3 and t>=1, the normal condition gives P_K(tw)=w,\n   so 2Phi(tw)=(2t-1)||w||^2=(2t-1)*2/3. The inequality\n   2Phi(z)<=2/3 cannot simply be imported outside K. This is a scope\n   failure, not a realized high-error trajectory on H_m.\n\nThese checks invalidate the two tempting extensions before either is used\nas a proof step. They leave a precise open direction: the actual\ntwo-dimensional triangle profile with no uniformly positive inactive\nfraction, or with unrestricted terminal amplitudes, needs a joint estimate\nof terminal value versus the complete initial transverse norm. The present\nbounded-terminal carrier proof supplies no such estimate. It also excludes\nneither a full simplex with no permanently inactive coordinates nor a rule\nwhose participating group union changes over the whole history.\n\nNo fixed two-group tuning, independent Cartesian inverse duplication,\ncoordinate creation/splitting, free carrier, expected face, unpriced extreme\ncoordinate, or function change was used. The scalar-amplitude lane remains\nseparate: the transverse process here has three noncollinear gradients.\n\n## 9. Retrieval, resource record, and handoff status\n\nSkills applied: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving, and\nconstruct-counterexamples. The active search was a mutation of the mature\ncentered-simplex inverse program, targeting all-history support rotation\nwith a fully paid radius.\n\nMatlas returned 1808.02681 Theorem4.1, 2012.00990 Proposition4, and\n1410.7012 Lemma36; none supplied the required GD mechanism or entered this\nproof. No formal submission is authorized.\nNo new assertion in this report is a verifier-accepted fact.\n\nThe proposed scoped answer is exact:\n\n\\[\n \\sup_{s\\ne0}\\frac{2\\operatorname{Hub}(\\Psi_m(s))}{s^2}\n       =\\frac1{D_m},\\qquad m\\ge1536.\n\\]\n\nThe supremum is attained at the actual initial amplitudes `s=+D_m` and\n`s=-D_m`. Its monotone radius envelope has a persistent plateau,\nand the plateau propagates to an infinite geometric family of radii.\nThese are symbolic inequalities, not executed trajectories, numerical\niterations, an amplitude grid, or an assumption that separately optimized\nchild ratios can be attained together.\n\n## 1. Original problem, scope, and accepted input\n\nThe original problem is unchanged at\n`/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/PROBLEM.md`.\nFor every finite nonnegative word `h` of length `n`, every `L>0`, every\nfinite-dimensional convex differentiable `f` with an `L`-Lipschitz\ngradient and an attained minimum at `x_star`, run\n\n    x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L) grad f(x_t), 0<=t<n.\n\nIts original full-class performance is the supremum\n\n    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2),\n    W_n^star=inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h), W=2R.\n\nNo zero-step, horizon, dimension, or word quantifier in that problem is\nremoved. Its target is `W_n^star/U_n^ZJ -> 1`, or a rigorous negative\nresolution with a full-function upper certificate. This report does not\nestablish either alternative.\n\nFor clarity, the original ALL-integer-split benchmark is retained:\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(integer 0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),\n    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nThese displayed recurrences are definitions and have not been executed.\nThe ONLY accepted mathematical input used for a quantitative benchmark\ncomparison is fact_id `931b9653ce0f7201`, read in full. Its unconditional\nstatements include `B_n<=(12/5)(n+1)^rho` and, at exactly the hierarchy\ndefined below, `B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50` for every integer `m>=1536`.\nIts separate `17/(16D_m)` full-W hypothesis remains unproved. An upper\nbenchmark comparison is never reversed to claim strength for a lower\nwitness.\n\nThe unverified finding `2993fddcc4a03577` was read in full as provenance.\nNone of its scalar lemmas or its copied-simplex reduction is an accepted\npremise. All scalar statements used here are derived jointly below.\nIn particular, the copied-simplex conclusion is NOT promoted by this\nreport. The earlier fixed two-ray family is not substituted for Hub.\n\n## 2. Fixed function, physical word, and elementary parameter bounds\n\nFix an arbitrary integer `m>=1536`. In this report the following hierarchy\nnotation is LOCAL, so the letters `a,c,H_j,A_j` do not redefine unrelated\nglobal glossary entries:\n\n    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, c=lambda(1+epsilon),\n    a=beta/c, d_j=a c^j for 0<=j<=m,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, n_m=2^m-1, D_m=1+2S_m.\n\nEvery child uses this SAME enclosing `m` and these same parameters.\nThe scalar `d_m` is an auxiliary radius ruler, NOT an extra physical\nbridge. The largest physical bridge in `H_m` is `1+d_(m-1)`. The original\nlast leaf `1+a` is present. Length induction gives `length(H_j)=2^j-1`.\n\nUse the ONE static function on the real line\n\n    Hub(x)=x^2/2                    if |x|<=1,\n           |x|-1/2                 if |x|>=1,\n    g(x)=clip(x,[-1,1]), T_h(x)=x-h g(x).\n\nAt both thresholds the definitions and derivatives agree. The derivative\n`g` is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz. Thus Hub is convex,\ndifferentiable, and globally 1-smooth. Its gradient has least Lipschitz\nconstant exactly1 on the interval `(-1,1)`. Its value is zero only at0;\nthe complete attained minimum set is `{0}`. The closest initial radius\nis therefore exactly `|s|` for initial state `s`, not a surrogate radius.\nFor arbitrary `L>0`, `L Hub` with the original physical steps `h_t/L`\nhas the same trajectory and the same W-normalized payoff.\n\nWrite `Psi_j` for the actual chronological composition over `H_j`.\nThus `Psi_0(x)=x` and\n\n    Psi_(j+1)=Psi_j o T_(1+d_j) o Psi_j.\n\nNo curvature, threshold, normalization, or function is changed during a\ntrajectory. Changing the fixed Huber threshold to `t>0` would merely\nreplace the initial amplitude by `s=x_0/t`, since\n`f(x)=L t^2 Hub(x/t)` has exactly the same displayed normalized score.\nThis observation introduces no additional dynamical law.\n\nThe bounds needed below are deliberately coarse and proved symbolically.\nHere `0<epsilon<=1/6144<1/100`, and `beta>99/100`. The identity\n`lambda(lambda-2)=1` holds. Since `sqrt(2)<29/20`,\n\n    2<lambda<c<(49/20)(101/100)=4949/2000<5/2.\n\nConsequently\n\n    1/3<99/250<a<1/lambda<1/2, 0<beta<1.\n\nLet `v=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)]`. The function `x(x-2)` is strictly\nincreasing for `x>2`; therefore `c(c-2)>1`, and `0<v<1`.\nThe exact mass identity is\n\n    A_j=(1-v)2^j+v c^j.                                  (2.1)\n\nIndeed `A_0=1` and summing the literal word gives\n`A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j`; substituting (2.1) verifies this identity because\n`v(c-2)=a`. Thus, for every `j>=0` in range,\n\n    A_j>=v c^j, d_j/A_j<=c-2<1/2, d_j<A_j.             (2.2)\n\nFor `j>=1`, both coefficients in (2.1) are positive and `2^j<c^j`,\nso `A_j<c^j`.\n\n## 3. Every original interpolation row and physical sample\n\nFor arbitrary fixed initial `s`, let `x_0=s` and run every original step\nof `H_m`. At each query, INCLUDING the terminal, define\n\n    g_i=clip(x_i,[-1,1]), y_i=x_i-g_i,\n    f_i=Hub(x_i)=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.\n\nAt the optimum use `x_star=g_star=f_star=0`. For EVERY ordered pair\n`i,j` among the physical queries, terminal and optimum, direct expansion\ngives the original signed interpolation residual\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2\n         =y_j(g_i-g_j)<=0.                               (3.1)\n\nTo check the inequality in all cases: if `y_j=0` it is equality; if\n`y_j>0` then `g_j=1` and `g_i<=1`; if `y_j<0` then `g_j=-1` and\n`g_i>=-1`. The optimum rows are `Q_(i,star)=0` and\n`Q_(star,i)=-y_i g_i<=0`. This includes reversed, remote, repeated-visit,\nand endpoint rows without deleting a query. The full Gram of `x_0` and\nall `g_i` is their ordinary scalar Gram and is positive semidefinite.\nThe recursion is the literal physical GD update on this single globally\ndefined function, so no interpolation-completion choice remains.\n\n## 4. Interior absorption and the exterior formula, jointly rederived\n\nFor every `0<=j<=m`,\n\n    |s|<=A_j  => |Psi_j(s)|<=1,                         (4.1)\n    |s|>=A_j  => Psi_j(s)=sign(s)(|s|-S_j).             (4.2)\n\nFor (4.2), a start with magnitude at least `1+S_j` has, before and after\neach step, its original sign and magnitude at least1. Each update\nsubtracts that step from its magnitude. This proves the formula and\nincludes equality at the boundary.\n\nFor (4.1), induction starts with `H_0` empty and `A_0=1`. All the maps\nare odd, so consider `s>=0` in the induction step. If `s<=A_j`, the\nfirst child outputs magnitude at most1, and the bridge has magnitude\nat most `d_j<A_j`. The second child outputs magnitude at most1.\nIf `A_j<s<=A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j`, the first child outputs\n`s-S_j>1`. After the bridge its signed state is\n\n    s-S_j-(1+d_j)=s-A_j-d_j in (-d_j,A_j].\n\nIts magnitude is at most `A_j` by (2.2), so the second child again\noutputs magnitude at most1. This proves the assertion for all inputs.\nNo step or final leaf has been removed.\n\n## 5. Exact symmetric-interval image: the new analytical mechanism\n\nFor any `d>0` and radius `r>=0`, let `E_d(r)` be the largest absolute\nvalue of `T_(1+d)(x)` for `|x|<=r`. Directly from the inner and outer\nbranches of T,\n\n                 d r,             0<=r<=1,\n    E_d(r) =     d,               1<=r<=1+2d,\n                 r-1-d,           r>=1+2d.              (5.1)\n\nFor `r<=1`, T is the linear map `x -> -d x`. For `r>=1`, the maximum\non the positive half interval is the maximum of `d` and `|r-1-d|`.\nOn `1<=r<=1+2d` this is `d`; beyond `1+2d` it is `r-1-d`.\nOddness gives the same maximum on the negative half. The three formulas\nagree at both endpoints. E_d is continuous and nondecreasing.\n\nDefine the full-word envelope\n\n    F_j(r)=max_(|s|<=r) |Psi_j(s)|, r>=0.                (5.2)\n\nHere `F_j` is local envelope notation, NOT a new objective function.\nEach Psi_j is continuous and odd. The image of `[-r,r]` is a compact\ninterval by continuity, is symmetric by oddness, and reaches its extrema\nby compactness. It is therefore EXACTLY `[-F_j(r),F_j(r)]`.\nThis proves, without losing correlations between the children,\n\n    F_0(r)=r,\n    F_(j+1)=F_j o E_(d_j) o F_j.                       (5.3)\n\nIn particular every value at the right side is realized by some input\nin the original interval; independent maximizing initial points for\nthe two children are not assumed. Each F_j is continuous and\nnondecreasing. Also (4.1)--(4.2) imply\n\n    F_j(r)<=1 for r<=A_j,\n    F_j(r)=r-S_j for r>=A_j.                            (5.4)\n\nFor the second identity, points with magnitude at most A_j give values\nat most1, and larger input magnitudes give `|s|-S_j`, increasing up to\n`r-S_j>=1`.\n\nEquation (5.3) is used for a symbolic proof. It is not the deliverable\nand has never been executed as a mathematical program.\n\n## 6. A plateau persists through every hierarchy level\n\nIntroduce one fixed scalar\n\n    b=a beta, so 0<b<1/2 and c b=beta^2<1.              (6.1)\n\nThe crucial physical-parameter placement is\n\n    A_(j-1)<d_j<A_(j-1)+2d_(j-1), 2<=j<=m.            (6.2)\n\nFor the lower bound at `j=2`, `d_2=beta c` and `A_1=2+a`.\nMultiplying their difference by positive c and expanding\n`c=lambda(1+epsilon), beta=1-epsilon` gives\n\n    c(d_2-A_1)=beta c^2-2c-beta\n              =2epsilon-lambda^2 epsilon^2(1+epsilon)>0.\n\nThe last sign follows from `lambda^2=2lambda+1<6` and\n`6epsilon(1+epsilon)<6(1/100)(101/100)<2`.\nFor propagation, `c-1/c>2` because `c>lambda` and\n`lambda-1/lambda=2`. If `d_j>A_(j-1)`, then\n\n    d_(j+1)-A_j\n      =(c-1/c)d_j-2A_(j-1)>0.\n\nHere we used `d_(j-1)=d_j/c` and `A_j=2A_(j-1)+d_(j-1)`.\nThis proves the lower bound for all later j. For the upper bound,\n\n    d_j-2d_(j-1)=(c-2)d_(j-1)<A_(j-1),\n\nusing `c-2<1` and `d_(j-1)<A_(j-1)`. This proves (6.2).\n\nNow the exact plateau height is\n\n    F_j(d_j)=b for EVERY 1<=j<=m.                       (6.3)\n\nAt `j=1`, `d_1=beta<1`, `F_1=E_a`, hence\n`F_1(d_1)=a beta=b`. At `j>=2`, (6.2) and (5.4) show that the first\nchild sends the radius `d_j` to\n\n    F_(j-1)(d_j)=d_j-S_(j-1) in (1,1+2d_(j-1)).\n\nThe bridge's exact envelope (5.1) sends that radius to `d_(j-1)`.\nThe second child sends it to b by induction. This proves (6.3) while\nretaining both complete children. The plateau is essential; replacing\nthe first child by an independently optimal radial constant would lose\nthis information.\n\n## 7. Geometric threshold control, including every intermediate amplitude\n\nFor EVERY integer `1<=j<=m` and EVERY integer `r>=0`,\n\n    F_j(d_j b^r)<=b^(r+1).                              (7.1)\n\nThis is a simultaneous induction in j with the universal quantifier\nover r retained. At `j=1`, `d_1 b^r=beta b^r<1`, so (5.1) gives\n`F_1(d_1 b^r)=a beta b^r=b^(r+1)`.\nFor the next j, the case `r=0` is exactly (6.3). For any `r>=1`,\n\n    d_j b^r=(c b)d_(j-1)b^(r-1)\n             =beta^2 d_(j-1)b^(r-1)\n             <=d_(j-1)b^(r-1).\n\nMonotonicity and the induction hypothesis at `r-1` give\n`F_(j-1)(d_j b^r)<=b^r<=1`. Thus the bridge is in the INNER branch\nof its envelope and has output at most `d_(j-1)b^r`. Applying the\nsecond child and the induction hypothesis at r gives\n\n    F_j(d_j b^r)\n      <=F_(j-1)(d_(j-1)b^r)<=b^(r+1).\n\nEvery input/output in this chain is a radius of an exact interval\nimage of the original word. No trajectory is replaced by a reset word.\n\nNow take an arbitrary REAL `s` with `0<s<=d_j`. Since `0<b<1`, there\nis an integer `r>=0` with\n\n    d_j b^(r+1)<s<=d_j b^r.\n\nThis selection is a proof for every positive real amplitude, not a\nfinite mesh or a grid test. It includes upper endpoints by the displayed\nweak inequality; if s is a geometric endpoint use that endpoint as\nthe upper one. Monotonicity and (7.1) give\n\n    F_j(s)<=b^(r+1)<s/d_j.                             (7.2)\n\nFor `d_j<s<=A_j`, (5.4) gives `F_j(s)<=1<s/d_j`.\nAt `s=0`, `F_j(0)=0`. In particular the following weaker non-strict\nbound is uniform on the whole interior, with no exception at a preimage\nthreshold:\n\n    |Psi_j(s)|<=F_j(|s|)<=|s|/d_j, |s|<=A_j,\n    L_j:=sup_(0<|s|<=A_j)|Psi_j(s)|/|s| <=1/d_j.        (7.3)\n\nThe proof needs no explicit enumeration of threshold preimages. It\ncovers all of them through the exact continuous interval images,\nendpoint-inclusive branch formulas, and a bound for EVERY real s.\nArbitrarily many repeated entries into and departures from the core\nare already present in the complete composition defining F_j.\n\n## 8. Origin and least-predecessor interface\n\nFor additional clarity, on `|s|<=1` the entire run is in the quadratic\ncore and the absolute final multiplier is exactly\n\n    P_j=beta^(2^j-1)/c^j, 0<=j<=m.                      (8.1)\n\nAt j=0 this is1. If it holds for j, then `P_j<=1`, and after the\nfirst child the bridge magnitude is at most\n\n    d_j P_j |s|=a beta^(2^j-1)|s|<=a<=1.\n\nThus both children and the bridge stay in the core. The new absolute\nmultiplier is `d_j P_j^2=beta^(2^(j+1)-1)/c^(j+1)`. This proves (8.1)\nand the core assertion jointly. Consequently the limit of the\nnormalized payoff as `s->0`, `s!=0`, is `P_m^2`. This is a finite-word\nidentity before taking any limit, not an exchange of m with a supremum.\n\nThe least predecessor magnitude for a terminal magnitude `u>=0` under\none physical step `1+d`, `d>0`, is\n\n    r_d(u)=u/d   for 0<=u<=d,\n           u+1+d for u>d.                               (8.2)\n\nIndeed core inputs give magnitude d times their input magnitude. For\nouter inputs of magnitude r>=1, the terminal magnitude is `|r-1-d|`.\nWhen u<=d, the core inverse u/d<=1 is no greater than the outer\nalternative `1+d-u>=1`; at u=d they agree at1. The other outer\nalternative is `1+d+u`. When u>d, the core solution is inadmissible,\n`1+d-u<1` is inadmissible, and only `1+d+u` remains. The case u=0\nhas least predecessor0. Oddness permits either terminal sign.\n\nThe map r_d is strictly increasing and left-continuous, with an upward\njump immediately to the right of d. If the chronological excesses are\n`e_0,...,e_(n-1)`, the least initial magnitude is\n\n    R_h(u)=r_(e_0) o r_(e_1) o ... o r_(e_(n-1))(u).      (8.3)\n\nThe rightmost map acts first; this is the ORIGINAL order of the inverse\ncomposition, not an unauthorized reordering. Monotonicity proves its\nminimality by backward induction: a larger required intermediate\nmagnitude cannot produce a smaller minimum earlier magnitude. At each\nstage the displayed minimum is realized, so this is an attained minimum,\nincluding threshold endpoints. No independent choices of child inputs\nare made. The hierarchy's palindrome is not needed for this argument.\n\nFor the present word, (4.2) excludes `|s|>A_m` from reaching terminal\nmagnitude `u<=1`. Applying (7.3) to every remaining predecessor proves\nthe evaluated, uniform inverse inequality\n\n    R_(H_m)(u)>=d_m u, 0<=u<=1.                         (8.4)\n\nThus all inverse thresholds, their preimages, the jump endpoints, and\narbitrarily small u obey the same paid terminal/radius comparison.\nEquations (8.2)--(8.3) are a rederived interface, not a second attempted\nanalytical mechanism or the unevaluated final answer.\n\n## 9. Exact optimization over ALL amplitudes\n\nThe interior payoff obeys, uniformly over `0<|s|<=A_m`,\n\n    2Hub(Psi_m(s))/s^2=|Psi_m(s)|^2/s^2<=1/d_m^2.       (9.1)\n\nThe equality on the left uses the terminal absorption (4.1).\nThe comparison with the Huber baseline is strict for the entire stated\nrange of m. From Section2, `a>1/3`, `A_m<c^m`, and `c>2`. Since\n`m>=1536>=5`, `c^m>2^m>=32>18`. Therefore\n\n    d_m^2=a^2 c^(2m)>c^(2m)/9>2c^m>D_m=2A_m-1.\n\nIn particular `1/d_m^2<1/D_m`. No large-m approximation is used.\n\nFor exterior amplitudes put `r=|s|>=A_m`. Formula (4.2) gives terminal\nmagnitude `r-S_m>=1`, so the exact payoff is\n\n    [2(r-S_m)-1]/r^2=(2r-D_m)/r^2.\n\nFor every r>0 the algebraic identity\n\n    r^2-D_m(2r-D_m)=(r-D_m)^2\n\nshows that this payoff is at most `1/D_m`, with equality exactly at\n`r=D_m`. The last equality g_n=x_n-z obeys the\nsame bound including z. Cauchy--Schwarz for n+2 terms, followed by summing\nthe n+2 original squared norms, proves (II.4).\nThus the first three terms of (II.3) are at least alpha80*Vnorm^2.\n\nAfter deleting its value terms, every individual Q row has absolute\nquadratic value at most (S+2)*Vnorm^2. For two finite indices use\n||x_i-x_j||<=S*Vnorm and ||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=Vnorm^2.\nFor an outgoing optimum row use ||x_i||<=(1+S)Vnorm, and for an incoming\noptimum row its quadratic is ||g_i||^2/2. The value terms of each cycle\ncancel, so the row bound can be summed inside that cycle.\n\nThe number of row occurrences over all cycles is exactly\n\n    Lcyc=3Nleaf(Nleaf-1)+(Nleaf-1)(Nleaf-2)\n                          +4(Nleaf-1)+3n\n        =4Nleaf^2+4Nleaf-5 < 2(n+1)^2.\n\nConsequently the absolute sum of all added cycle quadratics is strictly\nless than alpha80*Vnorm^2/2. Equation (II.3) proves\n\n    M_II(q_II) >= alpha80*Vnorm^2/2.                     (II.5)\n\nThis pays ALL cross-cycle and initial costs once, on the full space, for\nevery m>=1536. It is a full certificate draft at the very poor q_II. It\ndoes not suggest that cycles may be added freely at the desired tiny q.\n\n## 4. Required original-space screens\n\nUse exactly the earlier H4 direction: x_0=0, g_0,...,g_3=1,\ng_8,...,g_11=-1, and every other original gradient, including g_n, zero.\nThe two active blocks have equal physical mass, so their displacement\ncancels exactly. For Law I, (I.4) has all nonnegative terms and its\nfirst term is w80_0^I*b(2-b)>0. For Law II, (II.5) is strictly positive\non this nonzero original vector. These conclusions use the FULL forms;\nthere is no projection to a primitive or constant-gradient subspace.\n\nFor the deep wave at every 2<=j<=m-3, let p=2^j-1, Hbridge=1+d_j,\nx_0=0, g_0=-Hbridge/b, g_p=1, and all other original gradients zero.\nAgain b*g_0+Hbridge*g_p=0, exactly. In Law I the first nonnegative\nterm is w80_0^I*b(2-b)*(Hbridge/b)^2>0. Law II is positive by (II.5).\nBoth screens therefore PASS for both replacement bases, even if their\ninitial coefficient q is replaced by17/(16D): x_0=0 on both tests, so\nthat replacement does not change either value. Passing does not imply\na useful objective level; Section7 supplies a different test.\n\n## 5. Full initial/common-gradient and actual-terminal accounts\n\nFor either law, and indeed for any array on E80 with the correct flow,\nput K_ij=lambda_ij for finite i!=j, mu_i=lambda_(i,star),\nnu_i=lambda_(star,i), and h_n=0 as NOTATION only. The full polynomial is\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-sum_i mu_i<x_0,g_i>\n                                  +sum_(i,j)C_ij<g_i,g_j>,\n    C_ii=mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                    +h_i[mu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j.    (5.1)\n\nTo check the diagonal, half the total incident multiplier mass equals\nmu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n), by flow including nu_i. Forward rows out of i\ncontribute the additional -h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv. For i<j, squared gradient\ndifferences give the first term, forward rows out of i crossing j give\nthe second, and rows out of j reaching index<=i or the optimum give\nthe third. Incoming optimum rows have no initial mixed term. Thus no\nnu_i or displacement contribution has been silently omitted.\n\nLet e be the all-ones gradient column and Ppos80_i=sum_(t<i)h_t. Then\n\n    U80=sum_i mu_i=2+sum_i nu_i,\n    V80=e^T C e=D+sum_i nu_i(1+Ppos80_i).                 (5.2)\n\nFor a direct check, set g_i=1 and f_i=X-Ppos80_i-1/2. All finite Q rows\nand outgoing optimum rows vanish, while Q_(star,i)=1-X+Ppos80_i.\nSubstitution yields qX^2-U80 X+V80, proving (5.2). The initial/common\nblock is B80=[[q,-U80/2],[-U80/2,V80]]. In particular neither old Huber\ncontact derivative nor the old D/16 slack may be assumed for these bases.\n\nFor any variation v of the gradients orthogonal to e, put\nu80=mu^T v and k80=e^T C v. If Delta80=q V80-U80^2/4>0, exact\nelimination of the ONE initial/common block charges\n\n    [V80*u80^2/4-U80*u80*k80/2+q*k80^2]/Delta80.       (5.3)\n\nThe remaining quadratic is v^T C v minus (5.3). This formula includes\nall initial and common cross terms in one global charge. At Delta80=0\none must instead impose the range condition and use the corresponding\nsingular Schur complement. At the explicit q_I and q_II, the square\nidentities (I.4) and (II.5) directly establish PSD without assuming away\nthat condition; (II.5) in particular makes the common block positive\ndefinite. At the desired q, Section7 directly contradicts full PSD.\n\nLaw I has outgoing terminal probabilities1/2 at star and jlast. Its\nactual terminal square, from expansion of its rows or fact1252(c), is\n\n    M_I(q)=||g_n-Vlast||^2+Rlast,\n    Vlast=x_n-(x_(jlast)-g_(jlast))/2,\n    Rlast=q||x_0||^2-[f_(jlast)-||g_(jlast)||^2/2]-||Vlast||^2\n           +sum_(i!=j in {star,0,...,n-1})lambda_ij Q_ij.  (5.4)\n\nThe negative square cost in that prefix remainder is retained by (I.4)\nand (5.1); no terminal gradient is fixed by a quotient assumption.\n\nLaw II has incoming optimum-terminal mass n*eta80. Define\n\n    Aterm=1+n*eta80,\n    Bterm=(1+n*eta80/2)x_n\n                       -(eta80/2)sum_(i<n)(x_i-g_i)\n         =x_0-sum_(t<n)[h_t(1+eta80(t+1)/2)-eta80/2]g_t,\n    delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nIts exact ACTUAL-terminal completion is\n\n    M_II(q)=Aterm||g_n-Bterm/Aterm||^2+Rprefix,\n    Rprefix=q||x_0||^2+sum_(i!=j in {star,0,...,n-1})lambda_ij Q_ij\n                       -eta80*sum_(i<n)delta_i-||Bterm||^2/Aterm. (5.5)\n\nExpanding 2Q_(n,star)+eta80 sum_(i<n)Q_(n,i)+n eta80 Q_(star,n)\nproves (5.5): the terminal gradient coefficient is1+n eta80, its\nlinear coefficient is -2Bterm, and every f_n term cancels -2f_n.\nThe original prefix flow cancels the remaining -eta80 sum f_i.\nBoth initial and terminal costs are present. The probability formula\nfor an incoming-terminal-free certificate is deliberately NOT applied\nto Law II's different chosen support.\n\n## 6. A complete restricted-row transcript at every physical index\n\nThis construction tests ALL coefficient choices on E80, not merely the\ntwo explicit laws. It also shows why full boundary metrics cannot fix\nmissing rows by themselves. Define\n\n    Rreset=a*c^2/Mleaf=beta*c/Mleaf,\n    sbit(k)=number of ones in the binary expansion of integer k>=0,\n    v80_k=(-1)^k*Rreset^k*c^(-sbit(k))/Mleaf,\n                                      0<=k<Nleaf.                   (6.1)\n\nHere sbit(0)=0. This is a closed formula, never an executed recurrence.\nIf r=v2(k+1), binary carry gives sbit(k+1)-sbit(k)=1-r. Therefore\n\n    v80_(k+1)/v80_k=-Rreset*c^(r-1)\n               =-a*c^(r+1)/Mleaf=-d_(1+r)/Mleaf.        (6.2)\n\nFor each leaf index2k prescribe the REAL scalar jets\n\n    x_(2k)=Mleaf*v80_k,  g_(2k)=v80_k,\n    f_(2k)=(b+1/2)*v80_k^2.\n\nFor each queried checkpoint2k+1<n prescribe\n\n    x_(2k+1)=v80_k, g_(2k+1)=v80_k, f_(2k+1)=v80_k^2/2.     (6.3)\n\nThe leaf update gives Mleaf*v80_k-b*v80_k=v80_k. The following bridge update\ngives v80_k-(1+d_(1+v2(k+1)))*v80_k=Mleaf*v80_(k+1), by (6.2).\nThus these are the EXACT original physical GD relations. In particular\nx_0=Mleaf*v80_0=1; there is no freedom to change the radius in the proof.\n\nAfter the final physical leaf the actual terminal position is\n\n    t80=x_n=v80_(Nleaf-1)\n       =-Rreset^(Nleaf-1)/(Mleaf*c^(m-1)).               (6.4)\n\nThe sign is negative because Nleaf-1 is odd. Its binary expansion has\nm-1 ones. This gives (6.4) directly from (6.1), including the last leaf.\nComplete that terminal with\n\n    u80term=g_n=2t80/(b+2),\n    f_n=t80^2/(b+2),\n    x_star=g_star=f_star=0.                            (6.5)\n\nThere is no assertion of a single function interpolating these jets.\nTheir scalar Gram matrix is PSD because all vectors are literal scalars.\n\nWe now check EVERY row type in E80, including orientations and the\nterminal; no numerical check or finite-horizon inference is used.\n\nAt a leaf with parameter v,\n\n    Q_(leaf,star)=0, Q_(star,leaf)=-b*v^2.\n\nAt a checkpoint with parameter w, x=g=w and f=w^2/2, so both optimum\nrows and all ordered checkpoint-checkpoint rows are zero. For EVERY\nleaf v and checkpoint w, regardless of order or distance,\n\n    Q_(leaf(v),check(w))=0,\n    Q_(check(w),leaf(v))=b*v*(w-v).                    (6.6)\n\nThese formulas follow by substituting (6.3). They also hold with w=0\nfor the optimum. A neighboring checkpoint AFTER a leaf has w=v,\nso both adjacent rows vanish. At a checkpoint BEFORE the next leaf,\nv=-d*w/Mleaf with d>0; hence\n\n    b*v*(w-v)=-b*(d/Mleaf)*(1+d/Mleaf)*w^2<0.          (6.7)\n\nThese are ALL adjacent prefix incidences, so all of them satisfy Q<=0.\n\nPut u=u80term. Equation (6.5) gives\n\n    f_n-u*t80+u^2/2=-b*u^2/4.\n\nFor each queried checkpoint, including the optimum, its outgoing\nterminal row is therefore -b*u^2/4. For ANY leaf parameter v the\nterminal row is exactly\n\n    Q_(n,leaf(v))=-b*(v-u/2)^2<=0.                    (6.8)\n\nIndeed its expression is f_n-u*t80+u^2/2+b*u*v-b*v^2.\nThe remaining incoming optimum-terminal row is\nQ_(star,n)=-f_n+u^2/2=-b*u^2/4<=0.\nThis checks all row types(a)--(e), every optimum row, every leaf,\nevery checkpoint, and the actual unqueried terminal.\n\n## 7. All-depth growth, exact lower obstruction for E80, and failed target\n\nThis section is entirely symbolic. Since\nMleaf=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n\n    Rreset-1\n      =epsilon*[2/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda*epsilon]/Mleaf.\n\nWith epsilon<1/8 and lambda<5/2 the bracket is greater than\n32/45-5/16=287/720>5/16. Also Mleaf<5/2. Thus\n\n    Rreset>1+epsilon/8=1+1/(32m).                     (7.1)\n\nThe integer inequality 2^(m-1)-1>=96m^2 holds for m>=16. At16 it is\n32767>=24576. If it holds at m, then\n2^m-1=2(2^(m-1)-1)+1>=192m^2+1>=96(m+1)^2,\nbecause 96m^2-192m-95>0 for m>=16. This proves the assertion by induction.\nAll our m are in that range. Consequently\n\n    Nleaf-1>=96m^2>32m(2m+1).\n\nBernoulli's binomial inequality gives (1+1/(32m))^(32m)>=2. Together\nwith (7.1), this yields Rreset^(Nleaf-1)>2^(2m+1). Since Mleaf<4 and\nc<4, one has Mleaf*c^(m-1)<2^(2m). Formula (6.4) now gives\n\n    |t80|>2,\n    2f_n=2t80^2/(b+2)>8/(b+2)>16/7>2.               (7.2)\n\nThe exact expression, stronger than this convenient coarse bound, is\n\n    Qrelax80(m)=2*Rreset^(2Nleaf-2)\n                      /[(b+2)*Mleaf^2*c^(2m-2)].       (7.3)\n\nFor ANY nonnegative array supported on E80 with the required original\nvalue flow, evaluate its full polynomial on (6.1)--(6.5). Its initial\nnorm is1 and EVERY selected interpolation row is nonpositive. Therefore\n\n    M_cert(q) <= q-Qrelax80(m).\n\nFull PSD forces\n\n    q>=Qrelax80(m)>2, for EVERY m>=1536.               (7.4)\n\nThis is a support-level impossibility proof attempt: arbitrary weights,\nboth optimum directions, arbitrary context, arbitrary matrix boundary\npotentials, complete checkpoint couplings and the full terminal fan do\nnot escape it if their final row support stays inside E80. A free\nnonnegative square added to a storage derivation does not change this\nargument: after telescoping, the original-row certificate itself must\nstill be PSD and is tested by the displayed original vectors and values.\n\nThe requested q=17/(16D) is less than1 since S>=b>1 and D>3.\nThus (7.4) excludes it by a large margin for both mechanisms and their\nwhole specified coefficient family. This is not merely a bad choice of\neta80 or of the context weights. It is also not a contradiction with\nthe genuine full certificates (I.4), (II.5): their explicit levels are\nnecessarily at least (7.3), and the same calculation forces that fact.\nNo eigenvalue computation or automated recurrence was used to reach it.\n\nAt epsilon=0, Rreset=1 and (6.4) gives t80=-lambda^(-m). The growth\nin (7.2) specifically comes from the small positive SAME-m deformation\nrepeated across all leaves; it is not an objection to the undeformed\nsilver schedule's full certificate.\n\n## 8. Exact omitted prefix row and the missing boundary inequality\n\nThe transcript is provably NOT a legal primal instance. Let\n\n    pbad=n-2=2(Nleaf-2)+1, ellbad=0,\n    vfirst=v80_0=1/Mleaf,\n    wbad=v80_(Nleaf-2)=-Mleaf*t80/d_1.\n\nHere pbad is an actual queried checkpoint, and ellbad is the actual\ninitial leaf. Since t80<-2, d_1=1-epsilon<1 and Mleaf>2,\nwbad>2Mleaf>vfirst. Formula (6.6) gives the explicit forbidden row\n\n    Q_(pbad,0)=b*vfirst*(wbad-vfirst)>0.                (8.1)\n\nThis is a remote queried-prefix row, neither an incoming-terminal row\nnor a duplicate-gradient constraint. There is no way to infer its sign\nfrom E80: the complete transcript just checked is a counterexample to\nthat inference. The reverse row Q_(0,pbad) is present and equals zero.\nThis directional distinction is essential.\n\nOne economical new support direction is the nonnegative symmetric pair\nQ_(pbad,0)+Q_(0,pbad). It has zero value flow and acts positively by\n(8.1) on this transcript. But it is indefinite on the original formal\nspace. For ANY queried u<v its full polynomial is\n\n    Q_uv+Q_vu=||g_u-g_v||^2\n                 -<g_u-g_v,sum_(u<=t<v)h_t g_t>.      (8.2)\n\nSetting only g_u nonzero gives (1-h_u)||g_u||^2, which is negative\nwhen h_u>1; setting only g_v nonzero gives ||g_v||^2>0. Thus adding\nthis row pair is not a free PSD repair and starts a genuinely new\nunallocated coefficient mechanism. No such third mechanism is attempted.\n\nThe precise unresolved boundary construction is as follows. On the SAME\nHword_m, find nonnegative original multipliers with full flow and genuine\nremote checkpoint-to-leaf or other excluded prefix rows such that, with\nC and mu formed by (5.1),\n\n    C - (4q)^(-1)mu*mu^T >= 0 on ALL gradient coordinates,\n    q=17/(16D),                                           (8.3)\n\nor any other rigorously sufficient original-benchmark-beating level.\nEvery actual terminal and initial cost must remain in C and mu. Formula\n(8.3) is the exact Schur condition for q>0. It is a theorem-level missing\ninequality, not a technicality or an already constructed certificate.\n\nAt a minimum, any such law must supply enough positive action outside\nE80 on (6.1)--(6.5). (8.4)\n\nThis payment is necessary, never sufficient: (8.2) shows the costs on\nother original directions. A full solution requires one analytic all-depth\nlaw paying (8.3) while meeting (8.4), not unknown feasible SDP blocks or\nan unproved cone-invariance assertion. Allowing even all checkpoint\ngradients in the boundary state does not establish those missing rows.\n\n## 9. {"id": "7daa02069604529e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T17:13:50.297964+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max80 mechanism I, JOINT UNVERIFIED scoped failure: chronological optimum-sector storage with the exact final physical-leaf objective/gradient interface has every nonnegative original coefficient and an all-depth sum-of-squares identity.
